{{pageNumber,auto}}


{{wide



## Mastery System

*“Clarity over chance. Choice over chaos.”*  

---

The **Mastery System** is a roleplaying game designed for heroes who grow through struggle, discovery, and the choices they make at the table.  
It is not bound by rigid classes or endless charts of bonuses.  

Instead, it focuses on **clarity, cinematic action, and player-driven mastery** in a world where success comes not from luck, but from **understanding the system, making smart decisions, and taking responsibility for every choice**.

What sets it apart is how it handles power: **it is classless, modular, and mathematically transparent.**  
Your strength does not come from arbitrary level spikes or hidden feature packages — it comes from **Mastery Ranks**, **Stones**, your chosen **Trees**, and the way you combine them.

**Mastery Ranks keep heroes on the same curve.**  
No one falls behind because their class “comes online later.” No one runs away with the game because of a single feature spike.  
Progression is **consistent and comparable**, built on a foundation that can be **measured, tuned, and proven**, not guessed.

In the Mastery System, players are not at the mercy of hidden mechanics: they **see the numbers, shape their fate, and own their triumphs and failures alike.**

At its heart, the system rests on a few core ideas:

- **Attributes Define Capability**  
  Each point in an Attribute adds one die to its Pool. Attributes determine how much raw capability you bring to a roll, but they do not determine your Stones.

- **Stones Define Affinity**  
  Stones are gained through **Lifetime Experience** and may be assigned to Attributes or converted into Permanent Colorless Stones. They replace class features with a modular supernatural toolkit, independent of how high or low the linked Attribute is.  
  You decide where your supernatural affinity develops and what kind of hero you become.

- **Raises Create Drama**  
  You may always meet the Target Number for a normal success, but by risking more, by *raising the stakes*, you unlock special effects, crushing blows, and cinematic moments.  
  Each Raise is a gamble and a story beat.

- **Actions and Reactions Matter**  
  Every round is a choice: attack, defend, maneuver, or risk everything.  
  Reactions keep you engaged even when it’s not your turn — parries, dodges, counterstrikes, and warnings make combat feel alive.

- **Simple Dice, Big Moments**  
  Rolls explode. Success is clear. The math is fast, but the outcomes are cinematic.  
  You will know when you land a decisive strike — and when fate turns against you.

---

:
## The Spirit of Mastery

The Mastery System is built for **fast, tactical, and narrative-rich play**. It is a game where numbers represent potential, but choices define outcomes. 
You are not just rolling dice, you are gambling with destiny, bending your assigned Stones to fuel extraordinary feats, and carving your legend into the world.

---
In Mastery, **every success feels earned**, and every failure pushes the story forward. It is a system that rewards boldness, creativity, and mastery itself.


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide




### The Role of the Players
:
You begin as **scattered survivors** remnants of a fallen world, bound by fading memories and a spark of defiance.  
The game assumes you are not for the demons and that you would join if possible the last embers of rebellion struggling against the demonic order.  
Whether you fight for freedom, redemption, or your own survival, your story begins in the ashes of the old world.  

---
:
This is a **sandbox**, not a railroad. No one will hand you missions or tell you what to do. You decide where to go, who to trust, and what risks to take.  
The GM shapes the world’s response, but **you set the course** every decision carries weight.  

---
:
The world of Destroyed Faith is harsh and uncertain.  
Resources are scarce, allies are fragile, and every act of mercy or defiance matters.  
Killing a marked host destroys the vessel but not the demon, saving one may cost lives but can inspire hope in others.  
Your victories will be small, your sacrifices great yet even the smallest triumph proves that the darkness can bleed.  

---
:

> *“Hope is not given, it is made.”*  
> — Teachings of the Hidden Flame
:::::
::::
> ## Core Terms
> 
> **Pool & Keep:** Use only eight-sided dice (**d8**). Roll your Pool (X d8), keep the best Y.  
> **Exploding Dice:** On a natural **8**, **reroll that die** and **add** the new result. Repeat on further 8s. An attack designated for **Critical** explodes on natural **7s and 8s** instead.

> **Critical(X):** Before rolling a Martial Attack Pool or a damaging Spell Casting Pool, designate that roll as one of the **X Critical rolls** granted by the active Critical source. For that complete Pool, natural **7s and 8s explode**: reroll each qualifying die and add its new result; subsequent natural 7s or 8s explode again. One designated Attack Roll or Casting Roll spends one Critical roll, regardless of the number of targets in an AoE. Critical changes **Pool dice**, not Damage Dice. **Active Buff: Critical** grants X designated rolls per combat while that Buff is maintained; reactivating the Buff in the same combat does not refresh that allowance. **Agility Ability: Crit** grants 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 designated rolls **in the current Round** at Ranks 1 / 2 / 3 / 4, each with Critical(1). Applying two Critical sources to the same roll has the same 7–8 explosion effect and spends only the selected source's allowance. Disadvantage still limits a Pool to one exploding die.


> **Guaranteed Eight:** Before rolling, you may exchange **8 dice from your final dice Pool** for **1 Guaranteed Eight**. Each exchange removes eight dice and adds one Guaranteed Eight to the final Pool. A Guaranteed Eight is treated as a natural **8**: reroll it and add the new result, repeating on further 8s as normal. It remains a normal die result for **Pool & Keep** and is not automatically kept.

> **Target Number (TN):** The value you must reach to hit or succeed.

> **Evade:** The TN an Attack must reach to hit a creature. For an AoE Attack, one roll is compared separately against the defense of each valid creature in the area.

> **AoE Attack:** Roll once for the whole area. A Martial AoE compares that result separately against each creature's Evade. A Spell AoE compares the same Casting Roll separately against each creature's Final Spell TN.

> **Raises:** Before rolling, declare Paid Raises (+4 TN each). After success, each Raise adds +MR d8 Damage (AoE: +ceil(MR/2) d8) or +MR to an existing Special. Intellect Free Raises use the same options without increasing TN.

> **Penetration(X):** Ignores X points of the target’s Armor.

>**Armor (AR):** Reduces the rolled damage of a successful hit. Armor does not reduce Specials, conditions, ongoing damage, or other effect damage unless a rule explicitly says so.

> **Damage Reduction (DR):** Reduces all incoming damage of the listed type.
 
> **Action:** You may take 1 Attack Action per turn (Attack, Cast, etc.).

> **Reaction:** Special defense actions you may take outside your turn (Guard, Evade, Counterattack, etc.).  




}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### **Character Creation — Quick Reference**

Creating a hero in the **Mastery System** is about shaping a **unique legend**, not following rigid classes.  
Each decision defines **who you are**, **what drives you**, and **how you face the shattered world** of *Destroyed Faith*.

---

*Read the full chapter once, then use this to see that you have everything.*

---
:
#### **Step 1 — Choose Attributes & Starting Stones** 
*On your sheet: write your final values into the **Attributes** section, then assign your two starting Stones.*

---

> **Attributes** — assign fixed starting values: **4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, 2** across your seven Attributes.  
> Assign both of your **2 starting Stones** to Attributes; **neither starting Stone may be Colorless**. You may choose the same Attribute twice or two different Attributes. Attribute Stone assignment is independent of Attribute values. At MR 2, no single Attribute may hold more than **4 Attribute Stones**. Each permanent Stone earned **after character creation** may instead be chosen as a Permanent Colorless Stone at a **1:1 ratio** during an Upgrade Step.

---
:
#### **Step 2 — Select Skills**  
*On your sheet: spend your points in the **Skills** list and mark your final rating for each trained skill.*

---

> **Skills** — spend skill points; mark trained ratings  

---

#### Step 3 — Choose Powers

*On your sheet: pick your starting Powers and list them in the Powers section with their Power Level.*

> **Powers** —  choose **two Active Powers at Power Level 2**.  
> In addition, choose a number of **Passives equal to your available Passive Slots**, plus **one Reaction** and **one Active Buff Power** at **Power Level 4**. A normal **MR 2** starting character chooses **two Passives**; an **MR 1** starting character chooses **one Passive**. **Character-creation exception:** that starting Passive, Reaction, and Active Buff are granted at **PL 4** even at MR 1; the normal MR 1 **PL 2 cap** applies to other Powers and later advancement.

#### **Step 4 — Languages** 
*On your sheet: note your **languages** in the designated box or in the background section.*

---

> **Languages** — everyone speaks the **Common Tongue** and gains one additional language. For some Echoes, this additional language is fixed by their origin.

---
:
#### **Step 5 — Schticks**
*On your sheet: list your **Schticks** in the **Traits / Schticks** area with a short description each.*

---

> **Schticks** — **1 per Mastery Rank**

---
:
#### **Step 6 — Echoes** 
*On your sheet: choose your **Echoes** and record its traits and options in the **Race / Echoes** section.*

---

> **Echoes** — choose your Echo
---
:
#### Step 7 — Disadvantages & Reroll Points

*On your sheet: write your chosen Disadvantages and your maximum Reroll Points.*

> **Disadvantages** — take up to **8 Disadvantage Points**. Your total Disadvantage Points become your maximum **Reroll Points**.
---
:
#### **Step 8 — Finalize Your Hero**  
*On your sheet: calculate and fill in **Health, Fear, Defense, Initiative**, starting gear and a few key notes on appearance & background.*

- Calculate **Health** from **Vitality × 4** and record your current **Fear** (start at 0). Fear has no Health-style bars.  
- Note your **equipment**, appearance, background, and motivations  
- Work with your GM to weave your story into the **ruined tapestry of Tyhra**



}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


## Attributes 

---
:
Characters are defined by **seven attributes**.  
Each point in an attribute grants **1 die** to the related **Pool**, but attributes in the Mastery System are **not statistics**, they are **channels** of inner power.

---
Your attributes represent how deeply you can reach into the **divine spark** within you, your capacity to focus, release, and shape the energy of your own soul.

---

At character creation, no Attribute may exceed **4** and no starting Attribute is lower than **2**.  
Attributes determine the size of your dice Pools. **They do not generate Stones.** Stones are gained through Lifetime Experience and are assigned independently to Attribute affinities or converted into Permanent Colorless Stones.  
Later progression can push Attributes beyond 4, where raw capability grows increasingly exceptional.

---
:
### The Seven Attributes
| Attribute | Concept | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **Might** | Strength and presence of the body | How fully you can turn your will into force and motion. | 
| **Agility** | Reflex, balance, and flow | How purely you move with instinct and momentum. | 
| **Vitality** | Endurance and survival | How strongly your body can hold the spark of life. | 
| **Intellect** | Knowledge and arcane structure | How far your mind can reach into the patterns of creation. | 
| **Resolve** | Willpower and inner stability | How fiercely your soul resists corruption and bends reality instead of breaking. |
| **Influence** | Charisma and presence | How brightly your essence resonates with others and commands the world through emotion. | 
| **Wits** | Awareness and reaction | How swiftly your consciousness adapts and shapes events before they unfold. | 


---
::

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Starting Attributes

---

Every hero begins with **seven core attributes**:  
*Might, Agility, Vitality, Intellect, Resolve, Influence,* and *Wits.*

---
:
At character creation, attributes no longer use a free point-buy pool.  
Instead, assign the following fixed values across your seven attributes:

- **2 attributes at 4**
- **2 attributes at 3**
- **3 attributes at 2**

---
:
Each value may be assigned freely, but no Attribute may exceed **4** or begin below **2** at creation.

---
:
#### Example Attribute Spreads

The following examples show how the starting values **4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, 2** might be assigned to different character concepts.

These are not classes or fixed builds.  
They only show how Attribute choices shape your early dice Pools and combat direction. Starting Stone assignment is a separate build choice.

---
:
##### Frontline Fighter

**Might 4, Vitality 4, Agility 3, Wits 3, Resolve 2, Influence 2, Intellect 2**

This player assigns the two starting Stones to **Might** and **Vitality**. That assignment is a build choice, not a consequence of the Attribute values.  
They are built to hit hard, survive pressure, and hold the front line.

---
:
##### Mobile Skirmisher

**Agility 4, Wits 4, Might 3, Vitality 3, Resolve 2, Influence 2, Intellect 2**

This player assigns the two starting Stones to **Agility** and **Wits**. The same Attributes could have received different Stones if the player wanted a different supernatural focus.  
They rely on speed, positioning, reactions, and tempo rather than standing still and trading blows.

---
:
##### Spellcaster

**Intellect 4, Resolve 4, Vitality 3, Wits 3, Influence 2, Agility 2, Might 2**

This player assigns the two starting Stones to **Intellect** and **Resolve**. Attribute placement and Stone placement remain separate choices.  
They are built around spell pressure, resistance, rituals, and Stone options that support casting.


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Her Attributes

Alaris is built as a **mobile melee skirmisher**. She survives through movement, Evade, and Phasing rather than heavy armor, so her highest values support that identity.

| **Attribute** | **Value** | **Why Alaris chooses it** |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| **Agility** | **4** | Her primary combat Attribute. It supports Finesse attacks, Evade, and movement-based Skills. |
| **Vitality** | **4** | Gives her enough Health to remain in melee when an attack does connect. |
| **Might** | **3** | Keeps her dangerous with a greatsword and supports physical actions. |
| **Wits** | **3** | Supports awareness, tracking, and fast battlefield decisions. |
| **Resolve** | **2** | Gives her basic inner stability and supports several social and occult approaches. |
| **Influence** | **2** | Lets her participate meaningfully in social scenes. |
| **Intellect** | **2** | Structured analysis and academic spellcasting are not central to this concept. |

Alaris then assigns her two starting Stones independently: one **Agility Stone** and one **Vitality Stone**.

<!-- v0.9.9.0: legacy numeric character-sheet image removed; regenerate the asset with the compressed Attribute scale before restoring it. -->

{{note
##### What this example shows
Start with the character's intended playstyle, then place the fixed values where they support that playstyle. The lowest Attribute is not a punishment; it identifies what the character is not trying to specialize in.
}}

}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


## Skills

---
:
Unlike Attributes (which set your **dice pool**), Skills are a **consumable resource**.
They can provide a **flat bonus** added *after* rolling and summing your kept dice — **if you choose to spend them**.

Each Skill has a Skill Rating. Its maximum rating equals 4 × your Mastery Rank, up to a usual maximum of 32.
That Rating is also your **Skill Pool** for that Skill: the number of **Skill Points** you can spend from it.

All spent Skill Points return **fully** after a **Safe Haven Rest**.

Skill Points apply only to **Skill Checks**. They do not modify normal Attack Rolls, Evade, or Initiative.

---
**Formula:**  
**Final Result = (Sum of kept dice) + (Spent Skill Points)**

---
:

#### Spending Skill Points

When you make a Skill check, you may spend Skill Points from the **one Skill used for that roll** (e.g., Perception, Stealth, Medicine).

- You decide **after seeing the roll** how many Skill Points you want to spend.
- You may spend **0** points (no bonus).
- If you spend points, you must spend **at least your Mastery Rank (MR)**.
- You may then spend additional points in **steps of MR** (MR, 2MR, 3MR, ...), up to your remaining pool.
- **All-in option:** instead of MR-steps, you may spend your **entire remaining Skill Pool** for that Skill (even if it is not a multiple of MR).

Mark spent points on that Skill until the next **Safe Haven Rest**.

---
:

#### Skill Point Buy

Each skill starts at **0**.  
You receive **40 points** to distribute freely across your skills.  
**Each of these points raises a skill by +1. Later Skill increases use the Skill Costs table in the Progression chapter.**

- At creation, **no single skill may exceed 4**.
- Long-term cap: A skill’s maximum rating equals **4 × Mastery Rank**.  
  *Example: at Mastery 8, a skill can reach **32**.*

---


#### Example

A character is searching for signs of an ambush in the woods.  
The GM sets the TN at **22** for a Difficult MR 2 challenge.

He has **Wits 5**, **Mastery II** (keep 2 dice), and **Perception 4**. Because MR 2 requires Skill 4 for the full pool, he rolls his full Wits Pool.

He rolls **5k2** → results: 4, 6, 8, 2, 3.  
The 8 explodes → he rolls again and gets a 5, so the 8 becomes **13**.  
Keep the best two: **13 + 6 = 19**.

Now the player chooses how many **Perception Skill Points** to spend:
- If he spends **0**, Final Result = **19** (fail).
- If he spends **MR = 2**, Final Result = **21** (still fail).
- If he goes **all-in** and spends his full remaining Perception Pool (**4**), Final Result = **23** (success).

---



}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Skill Checks & Raises

All non-combat Skill checks are rolled against a **Target Number (TN)**.

---
:

##### Step 1 — Determine the **Challenge MR** (Target / Opposition)

Before choosing a TN, the GM determines the **Challenge MR**. This is the Mastery Rank of what you are trying to overcome, **not** the character’s MR.

Use:
- **Target’s MR** (creature, NPC, faction member, specialist, etc.) for opposed or target-based checks.
- **Creator/Owner MR** if the obstacle was made or guarded by someone (traps, forged documents, warded locks, hidden camps).
- **Scene MR** (GM-assigned) if there is no clear target: use the MR that fits the fiction (mundane vs trained vs elite vs legendary).

---
**Important:** Your character’s Mastery Rank does **not** raise TN by itself. Higher MR makes you **more reliable**, not that “everything becomes harder”.

---
:
##### Step 2 — Determine the **Usable Dice Pool**

Before rolling, determine whether the character can use the full Attribute Pool for this Skill. A Skill must be trained enough for the character’s current Mastery Rank.

**Full Pool Requirement:**  
Your Skill Rating must be at least:

`2 × your Mastery Rank`

Compact reference:  
**MR 1 = Skill 2, MR 2 = Skill 4, MR 3 = Skill 6, MR 4 = Skill 8, MR 5 = Skill 10, MR 6 = Skill 12, MR 7 = Skill 14, MR 8 = Skill 16**

If your Skill Rating is **at least 2 × your Mastery Rank**, roll your **full Attribute Pool**.

If your Skill Rating is **lower than 2 × your Mastery Rank**, roll only **half your Attribute Pool**, rounded down.

---

**Pool after reductions:**  
Use the actual dice remaining after Skill halving, modifiers, and penalties. A Pool may be smaller than Mastery Rank. Keep all available dice when the Pool is smaller than the normal Keep value. A Pool reduced to **0 dice** automatically fails the roll. Guaranteed Eight exchanges follow the Core Roll rule.

---

**Keep:**  
Normally keep dice equal to Mastery Rank; when fewer dice are available, keep all of them.

---

**Skill Points remain unchanged:**  
This rule only changes how many Attribute dice you roll. You may still spend Skill Points from the used Skill after the roll, following the normal Skill Point spending rules.

---

##### Example

A character has **Wits 8**, **Mastery Rank 2**, and **Perception 4**.

Because MR 2 requires **Skill 4** for the full pool, the character rolls: `8k2`

If the same character had only **Perception 2**, the Skill would be too low.  
They would roll only half the Attribute Pool: 
`4k2`

The character still keeps 2 dice because their Mastery Rank is still 2.

---

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
##### Step 3 — Set TN from the Challenge MR

**Baseline:** A skill check is Standard by default. Determine the Challenge MR (target/opposition/scene), set TN from that MR, then adjust by ±4. The character’s MR affects keep, not the TN.

Set the TN to **Standard (Standard TN = (8 × Challenge MR) − 2)**, then adjust the TN in **±4** steps based on the situation.

| Difficulty (relative to Standard) | M2 TN | M3 TN | M4 TN | M5 TN | M6 TN |
|----------------------------------|:-----:|:-----:|:-----:|:-----:|:-----:|
| Trivial (Standard −8)            |   6   |  14   |  22   |  30   |  38   |
| Easy (Standard −4)               |  10   |  18   |  26   |  34   |  42   |
| **Standard**                     |  14   |  22   |  30   |  38   |  46   |
| Challenging (Standard +4)        |  18   |  26   |  34   |  42   |  50   |
| Difficult (Standard +8)          |  22   |  30   |  38   |  46   |  54   |
| Extreme (Standard +12 or more)   |  26+  |  34+  |  42+  |  50+  |  58+  |

- Every **+4 over the TN** is **1 Raise**.
- For ordinary Skill Checks, Raises are counted **after the roll**. Every full **+4** by which the Final Result exceeds the final TN equals **1 Raise**. Raises are declared before rolling only when a specific rule explicitly says so.

---
:

##### Primary vs Alternate Attributes (Attribute Shift)

Each Skill has **one Primary Attribute**. By default, you roll the Skill using its **Primary Attribute**.
The only exception is Perception because we handle it special, because it is used so frequently.
**With the GM’s approval**, you may attempt a Skill using an **Alternate Attribute** if you can plausibly explain how your approach changes what you are doing (different method, leverage, angle, or risk).
**Mandatory Difficulty Increase:**  
Using an Alternate Attribute makes the check **harder by 1–3 Difficulty Steps**, **minimum 1**.

---
**Difficulty Steps:**
- **+1 Step (Close Fit):** A natural adjacent approach. Increase TN by **+4**.
- **+2 Steps (Stretch):** It can work, but it is clearly not the default method. Increase TN by **+8**.
- **+3 Steps (Hard Stretch):** Only with strong justification, special circumstances, or significant risk. Increase TN by **+12**.

---

##### Passive Skill Values

A Passive Skill Value represents what a character can accomplish calmly, carefully, and without immediate pressure.

Your Passive Skill Value is equal to **twice the relevant Attribute** used for the Skill. This preserves calm, reliable competence on the compressed Attribute scale.

`Passive Skill Value = 2 × Relevant Attribute`

If your Passive Skill Value equals or exceeds the TN, you succeed automatically without rolling.

Passive success should only apply when:
- there is **no immediate danger or time pressure**,
- there is **no active opposition**,
- failure would not create immediate combat or dramatic consequences,
- and the character has enough time to act carefully.

Passive Skill Values do **not** spend Skill Points.

The GM may still require a roll whenever the situation is contested, dangerous, rushed, unstable, hidden behind active opposition, or dramatically important.

If a Skill could plausibly use multiple Attributes, the GM chooses the Attribute that best fits the careful passive approach. Passive Skill Values do not use Alternate Attribute Shifts unless the GM explicitly allows it.

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Skills


#### Perception (Special)
*“Awareness is the thin line between hunter and prey.”*

---
Perception is the discipline of awareness, the ability to notice danger before it strikes and meaning before it becomes obvious.  
Unlike other Skills, **Perception can be rolled with different Attributes**, representing **three distinct lenses** of awareness.  
When you make a Perception check, **choose one Focus**. The Focus determines **what kind of information you can detect**.

---

:
##### Focus: **Wits — Immediate Senses**
You rely on instinct and raw sensory awareness: movement, sound, scent, silhouettes, and the feeling that something is *there*.
- **Best for:** ambush cues, hidden movement, sudden threats, “something is watching me”
- **Typical Raises:** poor lighting, loud crowds, rain/wind, distance, distractions, time pressure

---
**Roll:** Wits keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Stealth or Concealment

---
:

##### Focus: **Intellect — Patterns & Analysis**
You spot inconsistencies and logic: tracks that break in impossible ways, mechanical tells, repeating rhythms, and forensic detail.
- **Best for:** traps and mechanisms, false scenes, footprints and traces, “how this works”
- **Typical Raises:** complex environments, layered misdirection, partial evidence, unfamiliar tech/magic, rushed examination

---
**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Concealment (staged evidence) or Engineering (mechanical misdirection)

---
:

##### Focus: **Resolve — Presence & Occult**
You sense intent under strain: deception tells, predatory attention, hostile aura, and mental/occult interference pushing against your mind.
- **Best for:** reading threat intent, resisting glamours, noticing “wrongness,” social/occult pressure
- **Typical Raises:** fear effects, charm/glamour, intimidation, sensory overload, corrupted areas, sustained tension

---
**Roll:** Resolve keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Deception or Intimidation (social masking) or Occultism (occult/magical concealment)

---
:


**Focus-Gated Clues:**  
The GM may rule that certain clues are only available through a specific Focus.  
Perception answers *“what do you notice?”*, it does not automatically solve *“what does it mean?”* unless your chosen Focus supports that kind of insight.





}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### Physical Skills  
*“The body remembers truths the mind has long forgotten.”*

---
:
Physical Skills measure strength, grace, and the instinctive will to survive through motion.  
They are the language of muscle and reflex — the first art every creature learns.  
In **Destroyed Faith**, physical mastery is more than endurance; it is discipline born of hardship.  
Each movement Echoes purpose — a leap, a blow, a breath that defies the weight of the world.

---
:
#### Athletics (Might)
*“Endurance is the courage of the body.”*

---
:

Athletics is the measure of raw power and control — climbing crumbling walls, swimming dark waters, or holding fast when others fall.  
It is the strength that turns struggle into momentum and exhaustion into triumph.

---

**Roll:** Might keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Environment

---
**Typical Raises:** More weight, less equipment, speed, silence.

---
:

#### Acrobatics (Agility)
*“Grace is control disguised as freedom.”*

---
:
Acrobatics embodies balance, agility, and precision in motion — rolling under blades, running along rafters, or catching yourself before the fall.  
Where others stumble, you turn danger into rhythm.

---

**Roll:** Agility keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Environment 

---
**Typical Raises:** More weight, less equipment, speed, silence.

---
:

#### Stealth (Agility)
*“To disappear, you must first accept being unseen.”*

---
:
Stealth is the discipline of silence and shadow.  
It is more than crouching low — it is understanding sightlines, sound, and rhythm.  
True stealth is patience made physical.

---

**Roll:** Agility keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Perception 
**Typical Raises:** brighter light, faster movement, closer distance, multiple observers, open ground, while performing actions

---


---
:

##### Concealment (Wits)
*“Stillness is the loudest form of patience.”*

---
:
Concealment is the art of becoming part of the environment — of choosing where to vanish rather than how.  
It favors calm over motion, observation over speed.  
Hunters, assassins, and prey alike rely on its lessons.

---





}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

:
**Roll:** Wits keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Perception or Intuition  
**Typical Raises:** poor cover, shifting light, leaving no trace, hiding equipment, resisting active search, longer duration

---
:
#### Ride (Agility)
*“Trust is the rein that guides every mount.”*

---
:
Ride measures the connection between rider and steed — balance, communication, and unity in motion.  
Whether on horse, beast, or machine, mastery comes from respect, not control.

---

**Roll:** Agility keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Animal’s Instinct or Environment  
**Typical Raises:** obstacles or jumps, high speed, rough terrain, controlling panic, mounted combat, riding hands-free

---

---
:


#### Sleight of Hand (Agility)
*“A heartbeat of distraction is a lifetime of opportunity.”*

---
:
Sleight of Hand is dexterity refined into deception — nimble precision for theft, traps, and artistry.  
It demands speed, confidence, and a steady heartbeat.  
Those who master it can unmake danger faster than most can draw breath.

---

**Roll:** Agility keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Perception or Reflex  
**Typical Raises:** larger object, direct observation, time pressure, multiple items, planting or swapping, one-handed use

---


---
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
### Knowledge & Craft Skills  
*“Understanding is the oldest form of magic.”*

---
:
Knowledge is power remembered — fragments of what the world once knew.  
In **Destroyed Faith**, knowledge and craft are sacred acts: rebuilding meaning from ruin.  
Every skill of intellect, invention, or healing is an Echo of divine curiosity — proof that creation itself can still be imitated, even in a broken age.  

Knowledge & Craft Skills measure study, observation, and hands-on mastery of the world’s secrets.  
They bind logic to spirit, shaping the tools and truths that keep civilization alive.

---
:

#### Lore (Intellect)
*“Stories are the bones of the world.”*

---
:
Lore is the memory of all things — history, faith, myth, and the Echoes of forgotten tongues.  
It is both knowledge and reverence, for those who study Lore know that truth often survives only in fragments.  
To remember is to resist oblivion.

---

**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Deception or Uncertainty (GM’s call)  
**Typical Raises:** rare topic, ancient language, foreign culture, obscure religion, connecting fragmented sources, recalling forgotten names

---


---
:

#### Alchemy (Intellect)
*“Every leaf remembers what it was made to heal.”*

---
:
Herbalism and Alchemy combine intuition and precision: mixing, boiling, and binding natural essences.  
The difference between medicine and poison is often a heartbeat’s hesitation.  
Those who master this craft transmute suffering into remedy — or remedy into weapon.

---

**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Complexity or Purity of Materials  
**Typical Raises:** rare herbs, dangerous terrain, short time limit, large quantity, complex mixtures, toxic ingredients

---

---
:

#### Crafting (Might)
*“Creation is memory made solid.”*

---
:
Crafting encompasses the **hard, resisting materials** — wood, stone, metal, bone and the labor of shaping them through force, leverage, heat, and endurance.  
It covers blacksmithing, carpentry, masonry, heavy leatherwork, structural repairs, and all work where the material must be **bent, broken, welded, or carried** into place.  True craftsmanship borders on worship.

---

**Primary Use (Might):** forging, framing, roofing, felling, mining labor, structural builds, heavy repairs, toolmaking.


---

**Roll:** Might keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Material quality or Design complexity  
**Typical Raises:** fine detail on hard materials, poor tools, limited materials, large scale, structural precision, hostile conditions, time constraints


:


:

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide


#### Artisanry (Intellect)
*“The finest work is technique turned into beauty.”*

---
:
Artisanry is the craft of **soft goods and fine precision** — textiles, tailoring, weaving, wool processing, dyeing, embroidery, delicate leather tooling, jewelry assembly, pottery finishing, and meticulous decorative work.  
It is less about overpowering stubborn material and more about **method, pattern, technique, and control**.  
A true artisan does not force the world to yield — they persuade it to become elegant.

**Primary Use (Intellect):** patterns, measurements, technique, repeatable precision, complex designs, chemical/technical processes (dyes, treatments, finishes).

---

**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Pattern complexity or Material delicacy  
**Typical Raises:** fragile materials, ultra-fine detail, complex patterns, limited tools, rare pigments, matching symmetry, rushed work, poor light, contamination, time constraints

---




#### Engineering (Intellect)
*“Every structure is a prayer to logic.”*

---
:
Engineering is applied intellect — the design of machines, fortifications, and the bending of matter through thought.  
It is invention born from necessity, turning fear and faith into function.

---

**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Complexity or Environmental stress  
**Typical Raises:** complex mechanisms, unstable materials, siege scale, limited tools, environmental stress, time pressure

---


---

#### Medicine (Intellect)
*“To heal is to borrow time from death.”*

---
:
Medicine is the study of life’s fragility and the courage to oppose it.  
It demands both steady hands and a heart unbroken by loss.  
Those who practice it know the price of every breath saved.

---

**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery 
**Opposed By:** Injury severity or Disease virulence  
**Typical Raises:** severe wound, lack of tools, multiple patients, limited time, infection or poison, no rest conditions

---

---
:

#### Navigation (Wits)
*“The world is a map that only patience can read.”*

---
:
Navigation guides travelers through land, sea, or stars.  
It measures not only direction but intuition — reading wind, current, and omen alike.  
In an age where old roads fade, the navigator’s gift is as rare as faith itself.

---

**Roll:** Wits keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Weather or Terrain Difficulty  
**Typical Raises:** poor visibility, bad weather, no landmarks, magical interference, long distance, moving targets

---


---
:
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Occultism (Resolve)
*“The veil remembers every whisper.”*

---
:
Occultism is the study of hidden truths — the language of creation, the Echoes of gods, and the scars left behind by forbidden magic.  
Those trained in it know that power is not found in light or darkness, but in the space between — where will, word, and memory intertwine.  
To gaze too deeply into the occult is to risk being gazed upon in return.

---

**Roll:** Resolve keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Deception  
**Typical Raises:** ancient script, fragmented ritual, false leads, suppressed lore, divine or demonic interference, unstable magic


:

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### Social Skills  
*“The world is built on words — promises, lies, and the silence between them.”*

---
Social interaction in **Destroyed Faith** is more than conversation; it is conflict of intent.  
Every word carries weight, every gesture can wound or heal.  
Those who master presence, tone, and truth can shape empires as surely as any sword.  

 ---
:
#### Persuasion (Influence)
*“A steady voice can move mountains where force cannot.”*

---
:
Persuasion is the craft of turning intent into agreement.  
Through patience, empathy, and rhetoric, you reshape another’s perception without breaking it.  
True Persuasion is not domination; it is the art of guiding others toward what they already wish to believe.  
Those who master it can calm mobs, settle disputes, or change the course of kingdoms with a single promise.

---

**Roll:** Influence keep Mastery 
**Opposed By:** Deception  
**Typical Raises:** hostile audience, complex request, limited time, conflicting interests, large crowd, moral resistance

---
:

---
:
#### Deception (Influence)
*“Truth is only the lie we agree upon.”*

---
:
Deception is the art of misdirection weaving truth and falsehood until neither can be told apart.  
It is not always malicious: sometimes survival, diplomacy, or kindness demand a mask.  
Masters of Deception manipulate appearance, tone, and confidence until lies become indistinguishable from belief.

---

**Roll:** Influence keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Empathy   
**Typical Raises:** skeptical target, extended lie, partial truth, multiple listeners, prior suspicion, emotional manipulation

---


---
:
#### Intimidation (Might)
*“Fear is the most honest language.”*

---
:
Intimidation is the projection of dominance the force of presence that bends others without a single blow.  
It can arise from strength, certainty, cruelty, or calm inevitability.  
The truly frightening need not shout; they simply make others imagine what would happen if they did.

---

**Roll:** Might keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Leadership  
**Typical Raises:** fearless target, public setting, armed opponents, multiple targets, indirect threat, moral resistance

---

:

#### Leadership (Resolve)
*“To lead is to bear the weight of every step behind you.”*

---
:
Leadership is the ability to command not by fear or charm, but by conviction.  
It unites voices, restores courage, and transforms chaos into purpose.  
True leaders inspire loyalty even when hope falters, binding others through belief rather than authority.

---


---

}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide


**Roll:** Resolve keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Intimidation  
**Typical Raises:** chaotic situation, low morale, mixed loyalties, split groups, no visibility, high stress

---

---
:
#### Performance (Influence)
*“A lie told with beauty becomes truth for a heartbeat.”*

---
:
Performance channels emotion into expression voice, movement, or art that reshapes an audience’s hearts.  
From a whispered poem to a battlefield speech, each act carries power.  
The skilled performer doesn’t just entertain; they *move souls*.

---

**Roll:** Influence keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Empathy

---
**Typical Raises:** hostile or distracted crowd, poor acoustics, emotional precision, large audience, improvised act, extended duration

---
:

---

#### Streetwise (Wits)
*“Every city speaks. You just have to know which alleys whisper.”*

---
:
Streetwise is the instinct to read society’s undercurrents the ebb and flow of rumor, greed, and survival.  
It’s not about crime, but comprehension: understanding how people *really* live, trade, and hide.  
Those with Streetwise move unseen between castes and corners, fluent in the unspoken laws of the street.

---

**Roll:** Wits keep Mastery 
**Opposed By:** Deception or Authority  
**Typical Raises:** foreign district, high surveillance, secret networks, rival gangs, misinformation, urgency

---

:
#### Empathy (Resolve)
*“The eyes reveal what the mouth conceals.”*  

---
:
Empathy is the art of listening between words of reading the rhythm beneath speech and the tremor behind silence.  
It is not a trick of logic, but a sense for the invisible pulse that drives thought and emotion.  
Those trained in Empathy can feel deceit before it’s spoken, sense when faith falters, and glimpse the pain that shapes another’s choices.  
To open yourself to another soul, however, is to invite their shadows into your own.

---

**Roll:** Resolve keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Deception or Performance  
**Typical Raises:** evasive subject, strong emotion, conflicting signals, group interaction, deception involved, brief contact

---
---
:
#### Negotiation (Resolve)
*“A deal is a battlefield with smiles.”*

---
:
Negotiation is the craft of reaching terms under pressure trading value, managing leverage, and spotting hidden costs.  
It’s not only haggling for price: it’s structuring compromises, reading incentives, and locking commitments.  
Where Persuasion changes minds, Negotiation changes *outcomes*.





}}



\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


---
**Roll:** Resolve keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Negotiation (counterparty) or Deception (bad faith, hidden terms)  
**Typical Raises:** scarce goods, complex terms, asymmetric information, hostile counterparty, cultural norms, third-party pressure. 

---
:

#### Seduction (Influence)
*“Desire is a door — the question is who holds the key.”*

---
:
Seduction is the art of drawing someone toward you through allure, tension, and carefully chosen vulnerability.  
It is not “being pretty” — it is controlling attention, timing, and intimacy.  
A seducer reads longing, weaponizes curiosity, and turns attraction into access.

---
**Roll:** Influence keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Empathy (reading the play) or Resolve (resisting temptation)  
**Typical Raises:** guarded target, taboo or moral barrier, high-stakes setting, divided attention, prior history, reputational risk, magical wards


---
:

#### Investigation (Intellect)
*“Clues don’t speak — until you learn their language.”*

---
:
Investigation is the discipline of extracting truth from fragments.  
It covers crime-scene reasoning, pattern recognition, reconstructing timelines, and digging meaning out of documents, archives, and books.  
An Investigator doesn’t merely *look* — they connect what others overlook.

---
**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Deception (cover-ups, forged evidence) or Investigation (counter-analysis)  
**Typical Raises:** contaminated evidence, missing context, coded text, forged records, time pressure, supernatural interference, misleading testimony

---
:


#### Etiquette (Influence)
*“In the right room, one wrong word is a weapon.”*

---
:
Etiquette is mastery of formal society titles, rituals, hierarchy, and the silent rules of courts, clergy, and institutions.  
It lets you move through high circles without drawing blood you didn’t mean to spill.  
Those skilled in Etiquette don’t merely behave correctly — they *signal belonging*.

---
**Roll:** Influence keep Mastery 
**Opposed By:** Empathy (spotting a false mask), Deception (forged status), or Etiquette (rival protocol)  
**Typical Raises:** foreign customs, strict hierarchy, hostile court, obscure ritual, public scrutiny, high-ranking presence, political tension


}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
### Survival Skills  
*“The wild does not care if you live or die — but it will tell you how, if you learn to listen.”*

---
:
The world of **Destroyed Faith** is vast, untamed, and often older than its people.  
Civilization clings to fragile edges, while the forests, deserts, and seas whisper in tongues of hunger and silence.  
Survival is not only knowledge of nature, it is humility before it.  
Those who live long outside walls learn that instinct and respect matter as much as strength.  


::
#### Survival (Vitality)
*“The world provides for those who respect its cruelty.”*

Survival is the understanding of nature’s harsh balance - the quiet agreement between life and death.  
It teaches how to find shelter, warmth, and courage when nothing else remains.  
A true survivor does not fight the land; they learn its rhythm.

---

**Roll:** Vitality keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Environment 

---
**Typical Raises:** harsh terrain, lack of tools, group survival, extreme weather, limited time, scarce resources
:


---

#### Animal Handling (Resolve)
*“Trust is the oldest magic between man and beast.”*

---
:
Animal Handling is the bond of instinct — the ability to calm fear, guide strength, and speak without words.  
It is as much patience as skill, for no creature obeys one who does not understand it.

---

**Roll:** Resolve keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** Creature’s Resolve 

---
**Typical Raises:** frightened beast, injured animal, unfamiliar species, group of animals, combat conditions, limited time

---

:

#### Tracking (Wits)
*“Every step leaves a story written in dust and silence.”*

---
:
Tracking is the art of reading signs left behind — broken grass, bent twigs, disturbed air.  
It turns observation into narrative: where someone went, how fast, and what they feared.  
A good tracker can follow footprints; a master can follow motives.

---

**Roll:** Wits keep Mastery  
**Opposed By:** Stealth or Survival  
**Typical Raises:** mixed tracks, long duration, weather interference, false trails, night pursuit, high speed

---
:

#### Herbalism (Intellect)
*“The earth hides generosity beneath patience.”*

---
:
Herbalism is the skill of drawing sustenance from the land — food, herbs, and the quiet treasures of soil and bark.  
It is the difference between starvation and comfort, between poison and healing.

---

**Roll:** Intellect keep Mastery 
**Opposed By:** Environment   
**Typical Raises:** rare ingredients, hostile terrain, limited daylight, preserving freshness, precise mixtures, dangerous flora

---
:

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Weather Sense (Wits)
*“The sky remembers every storm.”*

---
:
Weather Sense is communion with the air — an instinctive reading of temperature, scent, and sound.  
To those attuned, clouds whisper intent, and the wind carries warnings.  
The wise never curse the storm; they listen to it.

---

**Roll:** Wits keep Mastery   
**Opposed By:** None (Environmental TN)  
**Typical Raises:** magical storms, rapid climate shifts, mountain or sea environment, long-range forecast, distorted air currents, unnatural silence

---


}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Example Skill Spreads

The following examples show how a character might spend their starting **40 Skill Points**.

At character creation, no single Skill may exceed **4**.  
These are not fixed builds or classes. They only show how Skills can support a character concept.

---
:
##### Frontline Fighter

**Athletics 4, Intimidation 4, Survival 4, Perception 4, Leadership 4, Medicine 4, Crafting 4, Acrobatics 4, Streetwise 4, Animal Handling 4**

This character spends all **40 Skill Points** across ten Skills.  
They are built to handle physical obstacles, resist pressure, protect allies, and remain useful during travel, repairs, and recovery.

---
:
##### Mobile Skirmisher

**Stealth 4, Acrobatics 4, Perception 4, Tracking 4, Survival 4, Sleight of Hand 4, Navigation 4, Concealment 4, Streetwise 4, Weather Sense 4**

This character spends all **40 Skill Points** across ten Skills.  
They are built around movement, scouting, infiltration, navigation, and surviving dangerous terrain.

---
:

##### Spellcaster

**Occultism 4, Lore 4, Investigation 4, Medicine 4, Persuasion 4, Empathy 4, Deception 4, Perception 4, Alchemy 4, Etiquette 4**

This character spends all **40 Skill Points** across ten Skills.  
They are built to understand magic, read people and situations, support the party, and solve supernatural or social problems.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Selects Her Skills

Alaris chooses Skills that reinforce how she moves through the world outside direct combat.

- **Movement and infiltration:** Acrobatics, Stealth, Concealment, and Sleight of Hand.
- **Travel and scouting:** Perception, Survival, Tracking, Navigation, and Streetwise.
- **Broader usefulness:** Crafting, Herbalism, Investigation, Persuasion, Etiquette, and Occultism.

Her high Agility and Wits make movement, awareness, and positioning natural strengths. Her smaller investments in social and knowledge Skills ensure that she still has options across exploration, investigation, and social scenes.

{{note
##### What this example shows
Skills should support the character's existing strengths and provide a few deliberate secondary options. They should not be chosen as an unrelated list of individually useful entries.
}}


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Choose your Powers

Powers are the abilities that define how your character acts in play.

---
They are not classes.  
They are not fixed builds.  
They are not prescribed roles.

---
A character chooses Powers to define what they can do in combat, how they survive, how they move, how they react, and how they express their mastery.

---

### Starting Powers


At character creation, choose **two Active Powers at Power Level 2**.

---
In addition, choose a number of **Passives equal to your available Passive Slots**, plus **one Reaction** and **one Active Buff Power** at **Power Level 4**. Characters normally begin at **MR 2** and therefore choose **two Passives**. If the campaign begins at **MR 1**, choose **one Passive** instead. **Starting exception:** the granted Passive, Reaction, and Active Buff remain at **PL 4** at MR 1. The normal **PL 2 cap** applies to other Powers and subsequent purchases/upgrades.

---

### Power Level

Each Power has a **Power Level (PL)**.

---
A Power's Power Level determines which level of that Power you use.

---
If a Power is Power Level 1, use its Power Level 1 entry.  
If a Power is Power Level 2, use its Power Level 2 entry.

---
Power Level is the only progression value needed for Powers.

---
Do not create a separate Spell Level, Tree Level, Class Level, or similar value.

---

### Power Types & Catalogues

Every Power belongs to one **Power Type**.  
Each Power Type follows its own rules, templates, limits, and examples.

---
The Core Rulebook only explains the general role of Powers.  
The external Power Catalogues are the rules source for building, reading, and resolving specific Powers.

---
Use the Power Catalogues when you need to know:

- how a Power is built,
- which templates are available,
- how Power Points are priced,
- what each Power Level grants,
- how examples are resolved,
- how Areas of Effect, Autofire, Split-Attack, and other multi-target techniques work,
- how Powers interact with Armor, Evade, Specials, Damage Reduction, movement, and action economy.

| **Power Type / Catalogue** | **Use it for** |
|---|---|
| **Actives** | Action-based Powers: attacks, healing, Specials, AoE, Autofire, Split-Attack, zones, barriers, and other direct effects. |
| **Passives** | Always-on or pre-selected character features: Armor, Evade, Damage, Health, Damage Reduction, Phasing, and passive Special support. |
| **Active Buffs** | Temporary combat states: stances, auras, offensive/defensive boosts, and maintained effects. Size-changing Transformations use their own noncombat rules; visual Form does not change mechanics. |
| **Reactions** | Immediate answers to specific triggers: defenses, counters, emergency protection, interception, and trigger-based responses. |
| **Movement Powers** | Movement replacements: dash, leap, teleport, flight, burrow, wall movement, phase passage, and similar movement options. |

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

{{note
##### Mentalist Option
A character may build around Mental Powers by choosing **Telepathy** as a Passive. Telepathy grants Telepathic Access and includes voluntary Mind Links at no additional Power cost. The character may then choose Mental Attack, Mind Illusion, Mind Probe, or Mental Control as Active Powers. A Mental Power's Power Level cannot normally exceed the character's **Passive: Telepathy** Power Level.
}}


---
:
### Spells

A Spell is not a separate Power type. **Spell is a delivery method** for an eligible Ranged Active Power.

The underlying Power keeps its Power Level, Range, Damage, AoE, Duration, Specials, action type, and base Technical Reference. Spell delivery changes how the Power is rolled and resisted.

A Ranged Active with Specials may use Spell delivery normally. A character may also have a number of **Special-less Spell Powers equal to Mastery Rank**.

A Spell uses **Intellect**, **Resolve**, or **Influence** as its fixed casting source. See the Magic rules for Casting Rolls, Spell TN, Spell Resistance, and the full Special-less Spell limit.

---

### Building a Character

When choosing Powers, think about what your character actually does during play.

A complete character usually wants some mix of:

- a way to affect enemies,
- a way to survive pressure,
- a way to move or control position,
- and a way to respond when threatened.

You do not need to cover every category.

A fragile sniper, a fortress guardian, a battlefield controller, and a mobile duelist may all choose very different Power mixes.

What matters is that each Power has a clear job.

For examples, templates, and detailed build guidance, use the external Power Catalogue documents.

---


}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Her Powers

Alaris begins at **MR 2** and uses the standard starting package: **two Actives at Power Level 2**, plus **two Passives**, **one Active Buff**, and **one Reaction** at Power Level 4.

| **Power** | **Power Level** | **Role in the build** |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| **Melee Single Target Attack** | **2** | Reliable direct weapon damage. |
| **Melee Single Target Special: Sundered** | **2** | Applies Sundered to prepare the target for later non-Spell attacks; this Power deals no direct damage. |
| **Passive: Evade** | **4** | Raises her everyday defense against direct attacks. |
| **Passive: Phasing** | **4** | Establishes Phasing at the start of combat. |
| **Active Buff: Phasing** | **4** | Reinforces that Phasing defense during difficult fights. |
| **Reaction: Phasing** | **4** | Provides an immediate answer to a decisive hit. |

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Powers.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

The two Actives give Alaris a choice between immediate damage and preparing a target for the group. Her defensive Powers then commit to one clear identity: **Evade handles ordinary pressure, while Phasing protects her from the hits that matter most.**

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Choose a Language

> All souls share the **Common Tongue**, born from the Echoes of the First War.  
> Every character also learns **one additional language**. For some Echoes, this language is fixed by their origin and cannot be chosen freely.

- **Dwarves** automatically learn **Dwarvish**.
- **Dragonborn** automatically learn **Draconic**.
- **Sentinels** automatically learn **Mustelic**.
- **Elorians** automatically learn **Elvish**.
- Characters of **all other Echoes** choose their additional language freely to reflect their origin, training, culture, or story.

---
::
#### Languages of the Known Realms

- **Common Tongue** – spoken by all civilized nations  
- **Arcane** – language of runes, sigils, and scholars of the old world  
- **Infernal** – the whispered bargains of hell  
- **Celestial** – hymns of the divine and long-fallen angels  
- **Dwarvish** – forged words of stone and steel  
- **The First** – ancient tongue of the Titan-born  
- **Elvish** – fluid and timeless, carried by wind and memory  
- **Draconic** – the primal roar of flame, wave, and storm  
- **Mustelic** – the precise command-tongue of Sentinels and divine law

---
:
#### Example

A Human with a demonic mark might choose **Infernal**.  
Their languages are: **Common Tongue, Infernal**.

An Elorian wanderer automatically learns **Elvish**.  
Their languages are: **Common Tongue, Elvish**.


}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Schticks
> *“Power leaves traces — in the body, in the soul, and in the world around you.”*

Schticks are **narrative signatures** created by the power awakening within a character. They may be physical, visual, audible, emotional, or supernatural: an unnatural eye color, frost gathering on the skin, a voice that carries a strange echo, sparks appearing during moments of anger, or shadows briefly moving against the light.

Schticks show **how Mastery changes a character**. They make growing power visible in the fiction, but they are not Powers, abilities, or mechanical upgrades.

---

#### What Are Schticks?

A Schtick is a distinctive trait that makes a character recognizable and shows how their inner power manifests in the world.

Schticks have **no mechanical effect**. They do not change Attributes, Skills, dice pools, Target Numbers, Damage, Armor, Evade, Movement, Senses, concealment, social rolls, or any other game value.

A Schtick may influence how the world is described or how NPCs react in the fiction, but it never grants an automatic bonus, penalty, success, failure, or special capability.

> **A Schtick describes what your power looks or feels like. It does not determine what your power can do.**

If a trait should provide an actual mechanical effect, it must come from a Power, Echo, Artifact, Stone Ability, or another rule that explicitly grants that effect.

---

#### Acquisition

You gain **one Schtick for each Mastery Rank** you possess.

A character therefore has:

| **Mastery Rank** | **Schticks** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1** | 1 |
| **MR 2** | 2 |
| **MR 3** | 3 |
| **MR 4** | 4 |
| **MR 5** | 5 |
| **MR 6** | 6 |
| **MR 7** | 7 |
| **MR 8** | 8 |

A character beginning at **MR 2** therefore starts with **two Schticks**.

When your Mastery Rank increases, choose another Schtick that represents how your growing power has changed you.

Schticks are normally permanent. They may change through major transformations, rituals, Artifact awakenings, Echo developments, or other significant events if the player and GM agree that the change fits the story.

---
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Progressive Manifestation

Schticks generally become more striking as Mastery increases. Early manifestations are subtle enough to be dismissed as unusual. At higher Mastery, the character may visibly appear touched by forces beyond ordinary mortal nature.

The following scale is a guideline for tone rather than a mechanical restriction:

| **Mastery Rank** | **Manifestation Scale** | **Example — Frost** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | A subtle physical or sensory peculiarity. | *Your breath mists even in warm rooms.* |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | A clearly unusual characteristic others may notice. | *Your touch feels cold and moisture briefly crystallizes against your skin.* |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Your power visibly marks your body or presence. | *Pale-blue lines spread beneath your skin like frozen veins.* |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Your presence creates small, harmless changes around you. | *Frost gathers briefly on objects you touch and cold lingers where you stand.* |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | Your nature becomes increasingly difficult to mistake for something ordinary. | *A halo of frost shimmers around you and tiny snowflakes sometimes drift through still air.* |

A new Schtick does not have to be an upgraded version of an earlier one. A character may develop several different manifestations representing different aspects of their Powers, Echo, Artifacts, experiences, or personality.

---

#### Using Schticks in Play

Schticks exist to help describe characters and their evolution.

Use them when describing how a character enters a scene, activates a Power, becomes emotional, suffers injury, invokes an Artifact, or reaches a new stage of Mastery. They may also become recognizable features that friends, enemies, witnesses, or legends associate with that character.

They remain narrative unless another rule explicitly gives them mechanics.

{{note
##### Schticks Are Not Powers

A Schtick cannot provide a free mechanical ability.

A character whose eyes glow in darkness does not automatically gain Darkvision. A character surrounded by frost does not deal cold damage. A character who appears briefly translucent does not gain Phasing. A character whose voice sounds terrifying does not gain a bonus to Intimidation.

If you want one of those effects, choose or acquire the appropriate Power.

**Describe freely. Resolve mechanically through the rules.**
}}

---
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Example: Schticks

The following examples show how different themes might manifest as Mastery grows. These are suggestions, not fixed lists.

#### Freeze

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your breath mists even in warm rooms. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | Your touch feels cold, and tears or sweat may crystallize briefly. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Pale-blue lines spread beneath your skin like frozen veins. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Frost gathers briefly on objects you touch, and cold lingers where you stand. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | Fine snow sometimes drifts around you even beneath a clear sky. |

---

#### Ruin

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your breath carries the faint scent of smoke. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | Sparks flicker at your lips, fingers, or eyes when emotion rises. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Thin ember-lines glow beneath your skin, especially around scars or veins. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Heat shimmer surrounds you, candles lean toward you, and ash drifts in still air. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | Your silhouette sometimes appears wreathed in distant flame without anything actually burning. |

---

#### Beast

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your eyes reflect light like an animal's in darkness. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | Your movements become unnervingly still, fluid, or predatory. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Teeth, nails, posture, voice, or facial features take on a clearly bestial quality. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Nearby animals become noticeably attentive, restless, or quiet when your power stirs. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | For an instant, observers may glimpse the outline of something far larger and more primal moving with you. |

---

#### Divine

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your eyes catch a faint blue gleam in darkness. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | A cold blue shimmer gathers around your scars, hands, or pupils when you focus. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Your presence carries a visible blue radiance, like moonlight beneath the skin. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | The air around you dims and glows blue; dust, mist, or falling ash hangs briefly in your presence. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | For brief moments, the space around you resembles sacred light from an age that no longer exists. |
}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Languages and Schticks

Every character speaks the **Common Tongue** and gains one additional language. For Elorians, this language is fixed as **Elvish**.

Alaris therefore automatically learns **Elvish** through her Elorian Echo, connecting her directly to Elorian history, communities, and surviving traditions.

Alaris is Mastery Rank 2 and therefore chooses two Schticks. Both are purely descriptive and grant no concealment, teleportation, movement, or defensive benefit.

### Between the Steps

Sometimes Alaris appears to vanish for the smallest part of a movement. An observer sees where the step begins and where it ends, but a fragment between those moments seems to be missing from reality.

### The Six Within

When Alaris channels one of her Powers, her eyes become completely black. Anyone looking closely can see the **Six Constants** burning faintly inside them like distant stars.


{{note
##### What this example shows
A Schtick should make a character recognizable without duplicating a Power as a free mechanical benefit.
}}


}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


## Playable Echoes
:

Echoes are **remnants of ancient lineages**, manifestations of divine, arcane, or elemental origins that survived the collapse of the Age of the Gods.  
Each Echo carries the **imprint of a lost world** its powers, its instincts, its flaws. To be an Echo is to be **a living artifact**: flesh shaped by memory, bound by purpose.

You can choose from the following Echoes:

- **Humans** – Masters of consequence: survivors who turn scarcity into leverage, make ugly deals, improvise with nothing, and pay the price later in Fear, fractures, and relationships.

- **Halflings** – The hunted hearthfolk: nearly extinct survivors whose warmth, memory, and soul-rich blood are prized as demonic delicacies. Most live on the run; others survive in locked cellars and feast-prisons, waiting to be served.

- **Dwarves** – The bastion against Hell: incorruptible stone-hearts who stand where others break, holding the line against demons, preserving what must not fall, and turning endurance into a weapon.

- **Elves** – A rare, burning glare: outsiders between realms whose light cuts through lies and rot, almost myth, almost painful, too bright to be common and too sharp to be safe.

- **Sentinels** – Forgotten fragments of divine order: living verdict-machines whose purpose outlived their makers, glitching through obedience, justice, and the hollow silence where meaning used to be.

- **Titanborn** – Awakened fury of the godless age: revered, wild front-line champions driven by inner rage, rising where the gods are gone to reclaim the world from demons with blood, steel, and will.

- **Dragonborn** – Hidden survivors of extermination: a people who endured genocide through secrecy and retreat, avoiding conflict because if the last of their kind die, the great song dies with them.

* **Unbound** – Those who chose freedom over purity: altered survivors who draw power from nature, forbidden craft, ancient relics, transformation, and traditions the gods once condemned. They are not a single people, but a loose family of paths united by one thing: they refuse to let gods, demons, or blood decide what they are.

  The Unbound include:

  * **Beast** – shapeshifters who embrace the animal nature within them.
  * **Witch** – practitioners of forbidden traditions, old magic, and coven lore.
  * **Bane: Alchemist** – hunters who harvest and refine natural materials to empower themselves against their prey.
  * **Bane: Greenwarden** – wardens bound to the living world, its creatures, and its untamed places.
  * **Bane: Relic Hunter** – seekers who recover and wield remnants of powers and civilizations that should have stayed buried.



}}


\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Her Echo

Alaris chooses the **Elorian (Elf) Echo**. It supports the same identity already established by her Attributes and Powers: grace, difficult movement, and remaining hard to pin down.

Her Echo Card is **Unseen Grace**. Depending on her approach, it may interact with Acrobatics, Stealth, Concealment, or Sleight of Hand. Her **Bounding Leap** Minor Expression reinforces the same theme by helping her cross small gaps, wall edges, and changes in height.

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Echo.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

{{note
##### Echo choice in practice
An Echo should support both the fiction and the ways a character approaches scenes. Alaris is a greatsword fighter, but her Echo also makes her distinctive during infiltration, travel, and exploration.
}}

}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Echo Artifacts


Some Echoes are bound to physical manifestations called **Echo Artifacts**.

An Echo Artifact is not ordinary equipment.  
It may be a body feature, inherited relic, living armor, supernatural organ, ancestral weapon, divine mark, machine frame, scar, scale, claw, horn, or similar manifestation.

Echo Artifacts follow the normal Artifact rules:

- They count against your **Artifact Capacity**.
- They occupy their listed **Equipment Slot**.
- They cannot normally be removed, sold, stolen, replaced, unequipped, or unbound.
- They progress through Artifact Levels like other Artifacts.
- They may grant Base Values, Artifact Functions, Stone Power Support, Active Buffs, Reactions, Movement Powers, Sense Options, or other written effects if the Artifact says so.
- A Sense Option is listed separately and never consumes Base Value A, B, or C.

A character normally has **4 Artifact Capacity**.

---
#### Choosing Echo Artifacts

Not every Echo handles Echo Artifacts the same way.

Some Echoes, including Humans and Halflings, begin with no fixed Echo Artifacts.  
Others must choose at least one Echo Artifact because their body or bloodline is defined by it.

| **Echo** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Human** | 0 | 0 | 4 |
| **Halfling** | 0 | 1 | 3–4 |
| **Dwarf** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Elf** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Dragonborn** | 1 | 3 | 1–3 |
| **Titanborn** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Sentinel** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Beast** | 1 | 2 | 2-3 |
| **Unbound — Witch** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Bane: Alchemist** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Bane: Greenwarden** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Bane: Relic Hunter** | 1 | 1 | 3 |

If an Echo requires Echo Artifacts, you must choose at least the required number from that Echo's available Echo Artifact list at character creation. If an Echo allows more than one Echo Artifact, you may choose additional Echo Artifacts up to the listed maximum. Each chosen Echo Artifact uses **1 Artifact Capacity**, unless the Artifact itself says otherwise.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Later Echo Artifacts

Echo Artifacts may sometimes be gained later.

This can happen through:

- Echo level progression,
- story events,
- awakening an inherited feature,
- recovering a lost body function,
- reforging an Echo relic,
- or explicit GM approval.

You cannot freely buy Echo Artifacts from another Echo. You cannot freely replace one Echo Artifact with another unless the Echo or GM explicitly allows it.

---



### Echo Deck

- **Each Echo has a deck of 4 cards.**
- **Start and Progression:** Choose **1 card** from your Echo Deck at Character Creation. Choose **1 additional card at Mastery Rank 4** and **1 additional card at Mastery Rank 6**. A standard **MR 2** starting character therefore begins with **1 Echo Card**.
- **Use:** Each card can be used **once per Safe Haven Rest**.  
  When you use a card, choose **one option** on that card (based on your unlocked Mastery tier).

---

##### Echo Roll 

When an Echo card triggers:

1) **Flashback beat:** 1–2 sentences of “ancestral memory” (the past repeating).
2) **Skill check:** Roll using one of the **Skills** listed on the chosen option.
3) **Base TN:** The GM sets the **Base TN** (usually **Standard**).
4) **Roll normally:** Attribute dice pool + Keep via Mastery; then optionally spend Skill Points after the roll.
5) **Count Raises:** **Each +4 over TN = 1 Raise.**
6) **Take what you roll:** Raises are not optional. On a success, you take all Raises you rolled. On a miss, you get 0 Raises.

---

:

##### Example (How an Echo Card plays):  
Oda’s **Halfling** Echoes triggers: he has to talk his way past a Sentinel patrol.  
He chooses the Halfling card **HEARTH TIES** and the option **II — FAIR TRADE** (*Skill: Negotiation*).  
The GM sets **TN 14 (Standard)**. Oda rolls as normal (Attribute pool, Keep via Mastery).  
He gets a **total of 25**. That is **+11 over TN**, so he succeeds with **2 Raises** (each full +4 = 1 Raise).  
Oda then spends **Skill Points** to push the result higher: he adds **+4**, raising the total to **29**.  
Now he is **+15 over TN** which becomes **3 Raises**.  

Because Raises are automatic, Oda **takes all 3 Raises** and applies them to the scene:  
- 1 Raise: the patrol accepts the deal  
- 1 Raise: they don’t search his bag  
- 1 Raise: they give him a tip or a safer route

If his total had been **15 or lower**, he would **fail**.





}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Humans

<div style="width:60%; margin:left;">
Humans are the most widespread people in the shattered world of Destroyed Faith. They are adaptable, resilient, and painfully easy to use. Where other Echoes fade into legend, withdraw behind old walls, or carry the burden of stranger blood, humans remain everywhere: in ruined villages, demon-held cities, broken shrines, prison caravans, and forgotten roads.

Once, they lived beneath the order of the gods, serving as priests, soldiers, builders, and keepers of daily life. Now they endure among ruins, rituals, and the weight of things that no longer answer. The cities they built have become strongholds of demons, tyrants, and false faith. Those who are not made into guards, laborers, vessels, or sacrifices learn to hide, flee, obey, or die.

Yet humanity is not easily erased. Again and again, humans gather in hidden enclaves, rebel cells, smuggler camps, and wandering caravans. They keep fires lit where they should have gone out long ago. They bury their dead, pass on forbidden names, and teach their children how to survive one more winter, one more patrol, one more night.

Many humans bear a demonic mark, burned into flesh by pact, force, punishment, or inheritance. Some marks whisper. Some wound. Some tempt. A mark may offer strength, but never freely. It is always also a claim a presence that presses closer with every moment of weakness, fear, or surrender.

</div>

Humans endure because they change. Some become willing servants of hell. Some search for lost rites, hidden truths, and fragments of dead faith. Others fight for smaller, fiercer things: family, warmth, loyalty, and the simple refusal to let the darkness take everything.



:

| **Human** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 0 | 0 | 4 |

### Halflings

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
Halflings were once the hearthfolk of Tyhra: gardeners, millers, cooks, ferrymen, peddlers, and keepers of roadside inns. They were rarely rulers and seldom conquerors. Their strength lay in making ordinary life endure, keeping food warm, paths remembered, children hidden, and strangers welcome long enough to become neighbors.

That life ended when the demons discovered what Halflings carried within them.

Something in Halfling flesh preserves the warmth of the old world. Demons call it **Hearthsweet**: a richness of blood, memory, comfort, and mortal joy that they cannot create for themselves. To many demons, a Halfling is not merely prey. It is a delicacy, a status symbol, and a reminder of everything Hell can consume but never truly possess.

The slaughter was systematic. Villages disappeared. Family lines were catalogued. Demon Houses built hidden larders beneath estates and fortresses, where captured Halflings were kept alive, carefully fed, and taken away one by one for private feasts. Some prisoners are found with old bites, missing fingers, ears, or pieces of flesh. Sampled and returned to their cells so that the rest may be saved for later.
</div>
Most Halflings alive today are fugitives. They hide beneath false names, inside wagon walls, under ruined floors, in root-cellars, sewers, smugglers' tunnels, and abandoned burrows too small for armored hunters. They travel in tiny family groups, rarely remain anywhere for long, and distrust any settlement with locked kitchens, feast bells or unusually well-stocked underground rooms.

Yet the old hearth has not gone out. Halflings still share food when there is almost none. They remember paths no map records. They can turn a hiding place into a home, a frightened crowd into a family, and a handful of survivors into a community worth protecting.

To play a Halfling is to be living proof that something gentle survived being hunted almost to extinction.  
You are small because the world made hiding necessary.  
You are kind because Hell failed to teach you hunger.

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Halfling** | Humanoid | Small | 8 m | 0 | 1 | 3-4 |

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The Hunger That Knows You

Demons do not forget the taste of a Halfling.

Once a demon has tasted a Halfling's blood or flesh, it can recognize that same Halfling again. Dirt, perfume, disguise, distance, and ordinary shape-changing may confuse the trail, but they do not erase the memory. The demon may dream of the Halfling, sense when they are near, or teach its servants the signs by which they can be found.

Even demons that have never tasted a Halfling may react to Hearthsweet when blood is spilled, terror breaks restraint, or a Halfling calls openly upon the warmth of their Echo. Hunger sharpens. Attention turns. A creature that had other priorities may suddenly decide that the smallest person in the room is the only one that matters.

Halflings therefore learn an unbearable discipline: survive without becoming memorable to the wrong monster.
