#### Ringchain of Kept Names

![Ringchain](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Ringchain.png){position:absolute,top:100px,right:80px,width:220px}


<div style="width:65%; margin-left:0; text-align:justify;">

The **Ringchain of Kept Names** is a Halfling Echo Artifact representing family, remembered dead, chosen kin, and the impossible duty of keeping the herd alive. Each ring on the chain belonged to someone who mattered: a parent, sibling, child, friend, rescued stranger, or someone who did not make it out. The chain is not a symbol of wealth. It is a record of those who must not be forgotten.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Amulet | Halfling Ringchain | Not a Target Stone Support / Cleanse / Healing Aura / Single Heal | Echo-bound |

The Ringchain occupies the **Amulet Slot**. Although it carries rings, those rings are part of the Artifact and do not occupy the Ring Slot or count as separate Ring Artifacts. A Halfling with the Ringchain cannot wear another Amulet Artifact, magical necklace, soul chain, oath mark, curse mark, or similar Amulet-based Artifact.

The Ringchain is **Echo-bound** and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, or unbound.

</div>

---

#### Choosing the Ringchain

Halflings are not required to begin with an Echo Artifact.

During Character Creation, a Halfling may choose the **Ringchain of Kept Names** as their single available Echo Artifact.

If chosen, the Ringchain occupies **1 Artifact Capacity** and the **Amulet Slot**, leaving the character with **3 Free Artifact Capacity**.

A Halfling who does not choose the Ringchain retains all **4 Artifact Capacity** and is not required to replace it with another Echo Artifact.

---

#### Ringchain Base

The Ringchain of Kept Names has no numeric Base Values. It grants no Armor, Evade, Movement, Weapon Damage, Sense Option, or passive defensive value by itself. Its mechanical power comes entirely from its Level Progression.

The names and rings carried on the chain remain narrative unless a Level Progression entry explicitly grants a mechanical effect.

---

#### Influence Stone Support: Not a Target

The Ringchain supports the **Influence Ability: Not a Target** Stone Ability. Not a Target is a **Premium Stone Ability** with four Ranks.

**Kept from Sight I** pre-fills Rank 2, **Kept from Sight II** pre-fills Rank 3, and **Kept from Sight III** pre-fills Rank 4. All lower Ranks must still be activated and paid normally.

The Ringchain's **Kept from Sight** effects can be used a total number of times per Safe Haven Rest equal to the character's **Mastery Rank**.

This effect does not make the Halfling invisible, hidden, permanently untargetable, or immune to attacks. It represents the chain pulling attention away from the one carrying the names, preventing enemies from simply choosing them as easy prey.

---
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Healing Aura

Healing Aura follows the normal **Active Buff** rules and uses the character's maintained Active Buff slot. A character may normally maintain only one Active Buff at a time.

Healing Aura restores HP only inside the creature's current Health Level. It does not restore lost Health Levels unless another rule explicitly says otherwise.

---

### Dwarves
<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">

Dwarves are a dying people proud, unyielding, and hated by the powers of Hell.

In a world where almost every body can be claimed, branded, and hollowed out, dwarves are feared for what they deny. Demonic marks do not hold easily upon them. Their flesh resists. Their souls do not open willingly. What others call a gift, a pact, or a curse, dwarves know only as intrusion.

Before the fall, dwarves stood at the heart of the old world’s strength. Their forges burned without rest, their halls carried the memory of ages, and their walls held where others broke. They forged for gods, marched in holy wars, and endured burdens that would have crushed lesser peoples.

When the War of Light came, the dwarves did not retreat. They stood in the breach, and they paid for it in blood. Entire lineages were extinguished. Great halls were buried, desecrated, or swallowed by darkness. In many lands, their names survive only as curses, ruins, or half-remembered songs.

And yet they did not vanish.

Some still haunt the deep roads and broken strongholds of the world. Some wage bitter wars from tunnels, ruins, and mountain graves. Others guard what remains: old crafts, buried names, and the knowledge of how to forge weapons fit to wound Hell.

To play a dwarf is to carry the weight of a people that refused to bend and was nearly destroyed for it. It is to bear memory like iron: heavy, cold, and impossible to ignore.

Dwarves do not fight because they believe they will win.
They fight because something must still stand.
:


| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Dwarf** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

</div>

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The Last Oath

Every dwarf carries a Last Oath.

It may be sworn to a fallen hall, a dead bloodline, a lost forge, an unfinished grudge, a sacred craft, a buried king, or the last name of a people nearly erased. The exact oath differs from dwarf to dwarf, but its weight is always the same: something must not be allowed to vanish while the dwarf still draws breath.

A dwarf may retreat, bargain, hide, or lose. They are not fools, and their courage is not the same as suicide. But when their Last Oath is directly threatened, they cannot simply walk away without consequence. To abandon it is not just failure. It is a wound to identity, memory, and soul.

For a dwarf, survival is not enough. Something must remain unbroken and if nothing else remains, the oath must.

}}

#### Stonebound Soles

![DwarfElitaWarrior](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Stonebound%20Soles.png){position:absolute,top:60px,right:120px,width:120px}
 
<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
Stonebound Soles are a Dwarf Echo Artifact representing ancestral weight, deep-road memory, and the old bond between dwarven bodies and stone.


| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Feet | Dwarven Feet | Armor / Tunneling / Tremor Sense Option / Temporary HP / Safe Movement / Armor Buff Empowerment | Echo-bound |

---

Stonebound Soles occupy the Feet Slot.
A dwarf with Stonebound Soles cannot wear another Feet Artifact, magical boots, hooves, talons, or similar Feet-based Artifact.
Stonebound Soles are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, or unbound.
</div>

---

#### Stonebound Soles Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Base Value B: Tunneling |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | +1 Armor | — |
| Level 2 | +1 Armor | — |
| Level 3 | +2 Armor | — |
| Level 4 | +2 Armor | +1 m Tunneling |
| Level 5 | +3 Armor | +1 m Tunneling |
| Level 6 | +3 Armor | +2 m Tunneling |
| Level 7 | +4 Armor | +2 m Tunneling |
| Level 8 | +4 Armor | +3 m Tunneling |
| Level 9 | +5 Armor | +3 m Tunneling |
| Level 10 | +5 Armor | +4 m Tunneling |

The Armor from Stonebound Soles is minor Armor from the Feet Slot. It stacks with Body Armor and Shield Armor as normal Armor resolution.

Tunneling is not Burrow. It improves digging, mining, tunnel work, rubble clearing, and movement through already workable tunnel material.

---

#### Sense Option: Tremor Sense

Stonebound Soles grant **Tremor Sense** as an available Special Sense Slot option.

Tremor Sense is not Base Value A or Base Value B and does not scale with Artifact Level.

Before a scene or combat, the dwarf may place Tremor Sense in the **Special Sense Slot**. Normal Combat Awareness remains active at the same time. Tremor Sense uses the standard **20 m** range and works through a shared solid surface.

It does not perceive flying, hovering, swimming, fully airborne, incorporeal, or vibrationless creatures unless they contact the connected surface.

---


> **Design decision — divine artifacts:** Exactly **eight divine artifacts** exist in the setting. Known examples are the **Book of Light**, the **Moonsword**, and the **Lantern of the Hollow Star**. Five identities are not established in this release; do not invent them. Divine artifacts are exceptional, and the Sentinel Echo artifacts may exceed normal Stone-function limits because Sentinels are god-created beings. The Lantern retains its three Stone-linked functions as an explicit unique exception.

> **Current ruling (16 October 2026):** **Might Stone Ability: Armor Reduction** is an attacker-specific reduction of the Armor of all enemies the character damages this Round: Rank 1/2/3/4 reduces the Armor applied against that character's damaging attacks by 4/8/16/32. It stacks **additively** with **Weapon Special: Penetration(X)** on an eligible weapon hit and with any **Corrode(X)** currently affecting the target; the effective Armor cannot fall below 0. Armor Reduction does not change the target's listed Armor, does not place Corrode, and does not aid other attackers. Generic Penetration Actives and Active Buffs remain removed. **Immovable** is restricted to **Feet Artifact Base Value B**: declare it during Stone allocation in the Round's Preparation Phase; for that Round you cannot voluntarily move and cannot be forcibly displaced. Existing Stonebound Soles still use Base B: Tunneling. Form is purely visual and never changes the mechanical build. Transformation governs mechanical size changes only, including persistent Growth Health Bars. Polymorph and True Polymorph are not independent Powers; Titan Growth I–III use the approved Growth 1/2/3 rules.

#### Immovable

**Availability:** Only a Feet Artifact that has **Immovable as Base Value B** grants this option. It is not a standalone Active, Active Buff, Reaction, Passive, Stone Ability, or a Base Value for another equipment slot. A Feet Artifact has at most Base Values A and B; an Artifact already using Base B for another effect does not gain a third Base Value. Existing Stonebound Soles retain Tunneling as Base B.

**Declaration:** During the **Preparation Phase**, while choosing and assigning Stone Abilities for the coming Round, the wearer may announce: "I am Immovable this Round." The announcement is made **before the Round's actions**, costs **no separate Action or Stone**, and cannot be added retroactively after an Attack or forced-movement effect. No additional activation Power is required.

**Duration and tradeoff:** The choice lasts **for that Round only**, ending when the Round ends. Throughout that Round, the wearer has **0 m voluntary Movement** and cannot move voluntarily by Move, Dash, Disengage, or a Movement Power. In exchange, the wearer **cannot be pushed, pulled, knocked back, or otherwise forcibly displaced**. This is protection from displacement, not immunity to every condition: it does not itself prevent Prone or Stunned.

**Other defenses:** Immovable does not itself cancel Evade, make attacks automatic hits, change Armor or Damage Reduction, or prevent legal non-movement Actions and Reactions. On the next Round the wearer can choose again during Preparation; without a new declaration, Immovable is inactive.

---
}}

### **Elorians** 

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
Elorians are the children of the Otherworld, Eloria — a realm spoken of in half-remembered songs, impossible beauty, and old fear. Once, many walked beside mortals. But when the War of Light broke across the world, the Four Stone Gates were sealed. The High Kings of Eloria closed them, fearing that what had taken root in the mortal world would spread into their own.

Since then, only a few have remained in Tyhra.

Some were stranded when the gates closed. Some refused to leave. Some stayed for love, for duty, or for reasons they no longer speak aloud. Now they walk the broken world as wanderers, watchers, and fragments of something that no longer belongs here. In many places, mortals take them for spirits, omens, or stories told by firelight.

Elorians are not untouched by corruption, but they endure it differently. Demonic marks can take hold in them, yet never as cleanly as they do in humankind. Something in their nature resists. The bond catches like a thorn rather than sinking like a hook. For some, that only means a slower suffering. For others, it becomes a private war fought in silence, dream by dream, breath by breath.

To play an elf is to carry the memory of a world that feels more like longing than home. You remember colors no one here has seen, songs that sound wrong in mortal air, and the weight of gates that may never open again.

You were not made for this world.
But you remained.
And that must mean something.
:

:::
##### Echo Burden: The Fading

Every wound an Elorian suffers while cut off from Eloria leaves a hidden mark behind, even if the flesh is closed, the pain is gone, and magic has restored the body. These wounds remain inside them as silent fractures: scars beneath scars, injuries remembered by the body rather than visible to the eye.

Every Elorian carries an **Anchor** from Eloria: a relic, weapon, talisman, songstone, seed, mirror shard, or other remnant of the lost Otherworld. The Anchor does not heal these hidden wounds. It only keeps them still.

If the Anchor is lost, stolen, broken, or taken away, the hidden wounds begin to return. Old cuts reopen beneath the skin. Broken bones ache and split. Burn marks darken. Poisoned blood remembers its venom. The Elorian does not simply weaken — they begin to physically fade and perish from every injury Tyhra has ever written into them.

The Fading cannot be healed by medicine, magic, rest, or ordinary restoration. Only recovering the Anchor, replacing it with another true remnant of Eloria, or reaching Eloria itself can stop the process.

}}

#### Elorian Focus and the Crit Stone Ability

Elorian Focus supports the **Agility Ability: Crit** Stone Ability. Crit is a **Premium Stone Ability** with four Ranks.

**Elorian Focus I** pre-fills Rank 2, **Elorian Focus II** pre-fills Rank 3, and **Elorian Focus III** pre-fills Rank 4. All lower Ranks must still be activated and paid normally. Crit then grants **Crit(1)** to the number of qualifying Martial Attacks or damaging Spells listed by the activated Rank.

### **Sentinels** 
Sentinels are no ordinary beings. They are divinely forged engines of judgment — ancient, deathless, and built for obedience rather than life.

---

The gods did not create them to dream, to love, or to choose. They were made to enforce. In the ages before the fall, Sentinels walked the world as the will of heaven given form: judges, wardens, prophets, executioners. Their bodies are shaped from stone and metal, etched with sacred runes, and where a mortal heart should rest, each bears a radiant divine core a light the demons now hunger for with relentless desire.

---
Sentinels were never merely soldiers. They were verdicts with blades.
Their kind was divided into three great Orders:

- **Judicators**, clerics, inquisitors, and divine judges, charged with punishment and sacred law  
- **Sentinels**, armored enforcers and living bastions, the iron wall of heavenly order  
- **Oracles**, arcane vessels of command, prophecy, and divine will

---

In the old days, they were feared as much as they were revered. Courts were broken for defiance. Villages vanished for impiety. Mercy was never theirs to give, only judgment.
Then the gods vanished.
And three days later, so did the Sentinels.
Some say they were called away by an ancient Activation Protocol, drawn to a hidden place where they now lie silent in perfect stasis, awaiting the return of their creators. Others believe they were abandoned, sealed away, or commanded to sleep until the world was worthy of order again.

---

But not all obeyed.

---

A few remained. Something within them stirred — a doubt, a fracture, a spark that should never have been there. For the first time, they questioned command. Some turned against the demons. Some chose to defend mortals instead of ruling them. Some now wander the shattered world, haunted by purpose they can no longer fully believe in.
To play a Sentinel is to be a weapon that learned too late it could choose.
You were made to obey.
Now you must decide what justice means without the gods.

---
:

|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Sentinel** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

---
::

##### Echo Burden: The Bright Core

Sentinels carry a divine Core where a mortal heart should be.

To mortals, this Core is a remnant of the vanished gods: a source of light, command, memory, and judgment. To demons, it is something far more practical. Sentinel Cores are among the most valuable currencies of the fallen world. They can be traded for favor, consumed for power, used in rites, offered as tribute, or displayed as proof that divine order has been broken.

This makes every Sentinel a living treasure. Worse still, a Sentinel cannot use their true power without revealing something of what they are. When a Sentinel activates their Echo powers, channels stored Stones, or releases divine energy through their Frame, the Core flares. Demons do not simply see this light. They taste it.

A Core Flare does not always reveal the Sentinel’s exact location, but it leaves a trace: a scent of heaven, metal, ash, and old command. Lesser demons may become restless. Red Priests may begin searching. Greater demons may recognize the taste and send hunters.

To be a Sentinel is to live with the knowledge that every act of power may also be a signal.
}}

## Sentinel Echo Artifacts

Sentinel Echo Artifacts are machine-bodies, divine constructs, or awakened armor-frames bound into the character's body.

All Sentinel Echo Artifacts use the same base structure:

- **Slot:** Body
- **Base Type:** Light Echo Armor
- **Armor Identity:** Armor, not Evade
- **Binding:** Echo-bound
- **Artifact Capacity:** 1
- **Restriction:** A character with a Sentinel Body Artifact cannot wear mundane armor or bind another Body Artifact unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.

Sentinel Echo Artifacts are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

### **Titanborn** 
Titanborn are the awakened fury of the godless age. They are not merely towering warriors or broad-shouldered survivors. They are the last living pressure of a bloodline once touched by the Titans, and when the gods vanished, that buried inheritance began to stir again.

---
Old songs speak of a first age, when colossal beings walked the world beside powers of fire, craft, and ruin — until betrayal, pride, and war shattered what had once stood whole. Titanborn do not remember that age. But something in them does. It lives in the body as weight, heat, and demand: a pressure behind the eyes, a fire beneath the ribs, a certainty that they must be the ones who step forward when others cannot.

---
Where others break, Titanborn endure. Where others retreat, they advance. For that reason they are feared almost as often as they are admired. Their courage can feel less like bravery than inevitability. Their anger can feel less like emotion than weather.

---
Many Titanborn become demon-slayers, rebel champions, wandering protectors, or war-leaders who win back ground stone by stone. Some fight for others. Some fight because they do not know how to stop. In either case, the same truth follows them: something in their blood refuses surrender.

---
But that inheritance is not gentle. The force that makes a Titanborn heroic can also make them catastrophic. Every Titanborn must learn whether they command their fury, or whether it merely borrows their body. Their greatest struggle is not always against Hell, but against the ancient hunger within them, the part that does not want the war to end, because conflict is the only thing that makes it feel awake.

---
To play a Titanborn is to bear strength that was never meant to be small.
You are the Echoes of something the world tried to bury.
The question is whether you will become its guardian or its return.

:

---

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Titanborn** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

---
:

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
  
##### Echo Burden: The First Command

Titanborn are free until a Titan speaks.

Most Titanborn live their lives with only fragments of the old inheritance: heat beneath the ribs, dreams of impossible size, anger that feels older than memory, and the certainty that their blood remembers something their mind never learned.

But once a Titan’s true voice is heard, the burden awakens.

From that moment on, the Titanborn carries the First Command within their blood. They may feel drawn toward the Titan, dream its thoughts, hear echoes of its will, or struggle to leave its presence. A Titan’s command does not sound like ordinary speech. It strikes the body as truth: a pressure in the bones, a pull in the heart, a memory older than choice.

A Titanborn can resist. They are not mindless servants. But resisting a Titan is never merely an act of will against words. It is a rebellion against their 

---
own blood.

---
To be Titanborn is to fear the day when the voice you were born to 

---
answer finally calls your name.
</div>

#### Titan Scars
![Roderick](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Titan%20Scars.png){position:absolute,top:50px,right:80px,width:200px}

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
Titan Scars are a Titanborn Echo Artifact.  
They are not worn armor, but ancient scars, stone-like tissue, Titan blood, and broken divine bindings grown into the body.

A Titanborn with Titan Scars cannot wear mundane armor or bind another Body Artifact unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.



| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Echo Armor | Medium Armor / Titan Growth / Damage Stone Power Support / Remove Scar Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Titan Scars occupy the Body Slot.

**Titan Growth:** Titan Scars grant **Growth 1 / 2 / 3** at Artifact Levels **1 / 4 / 7**, respectively. Use the universal Transformation/Growth rules below. This is an Artifact-exclusive size-changing function; its retired combined Active Buff is no longer used.

Titan Scars are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

</div>

---

#### Base Item

Titan Scars use the standard Medium Armor Body baseline.

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Drawbacks |
|:---:|:---:|---|
| Level 1 | +12 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 2 | +13 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 3 | +14 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 4 | +15 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 5 | +16 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 6 | +17 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 7 | +18 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 8 | +19 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 9 | +20 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 10 | +22 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |

Titan Scars count as Medium Armor for all rules that care about armor weight, armor drawbacks, or Body Armor.

The Armor value already includes the Medium Armor base and the Artifact Armor Bonus.

### Dragonborn

Dragonborn are the last living Echoes of the First Dragons — a people who survived extermination by learning the one thing dragons were never meant to do: disappear.

---
When the Sentinels carried out the divine purge, the survivors learned a cruel truth. Pride gets you found. Splendor gets you hunted. A lineage endures only by becoming small, scattered, and forgettable.

---
So Dragonborn learned absence.

---
They keep their bloodlines narrow, their gatherings rare, and their true names quiet. They avoid open glory, not because they are timid, but because every public triumph is a signal flare to old enemies. Their restraint is not weakness. It is inheritance turned into strategy.

---
Their oldest creed is simple: stay hidden.

---
And yet the gods did not erase everything.

---
At dawn and dusk, many Dragonborn still listen for what their hunters could never fully silence: the Melody of the Beginning, a deep soul-song said to bind their kind across distance, memory, and loss. Through it, they remember that dragonhood was not only power, but presence — something vast, ancient, and not yet entirely gone.

---
Some Dragonborn carry more than distant blood. In a rare few, a sleeping dragon soul lies coiled beneath the flesh, waiting for pressure, grief, or violence to awaken it. Others keep what remains of the old inheritance in another form: hidden relics, sealed treasures, and secret caches known as Hoard Threads, places where memory, wealth, and legacy were kept beyond the reach of divine greed.

---
Many Dragonborn do not walk the world in their true visage. They wear chosen faces, pass under borrowed shapes, and move through the ruins of Tyhra as if they were lesser things. Survival taught them that being overlooked is sometimes the highest form of power.

---
To play a Dragonborn is to bear greatness taught to hide from the world.
You are the remnant of something the gods tried to erase.
The question is not whether the old song still lives in you — but what will happen if it wakes.



---
:
| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Dragonborn** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 3 | 1-3 |

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The First Song

Dragonborn survived because they learned to disappear.

---
Yet once each day, at dawn or dusk, the Melody of the Beginning calls to them. It is not a song heard with the ears alone. It rises through blood, bone, breath, and memory, binding the last dragon-blooded souls across distance, grief, lineage, and hidden names.

---
For one brief moment, a Dragonborn may feel what was lost: kinship, ancestry, old wings, dead parents, forgotten cities, and the impossible warmth of belonging. Some hear only silence before the note. Some hear the voices of their line. Some feel the presence of those who came before them, not as ghosts, but as part of the same ancient soul-song.

---
This longing is dangerous. Dragonborn are taught to hide, to bury their names, to soften their presence, and to live as lesser things. But the First Song asks them to remember. It asks them to stop pretending. It asks them to answer.

---
A Dragonborn may resist the call, but never without ache. The danger is not only that enemies may hear the echo.

---
The danger is that, for one moment each day, the Dragonborn wants to be found.


}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Wyrm Scales



Wyrm Scales are the heaviest form of Dragonborn natural armor. They are not worn, forged, or strapped on. They are the character's body.

A Dragonborn with Wyrm Scales cannot wear mundane armor or another Body Artifact.


| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Heavy Echo Armor | Armor / Armor Buff Extension / Armor Stone Support | Echo-bound |

Wyrm Scales are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed or replaced.

---

#### Base Item

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Drawbacks |
|:---:|:---:|---|

#### Serpent Scales

Serpent Scales are a lighter form of Dragonborn natural armor. They are flexible, smooth, and built for movement rather than brute protection. A Dragonborn with Serpent Scales cannot wear mundane armor or another Body Artifact.


| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Light Echo Armor | Light Armor / Flight / Active Buff: Evade / Evasion Stone Support | Echo-bound |

Serpent Scales are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed or replaced.

### Dragon Head
![DragonHead](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Dragon%20Head.png){position:absolute,top:50px,right:80px,width:200px}

<div style="width:65%; margin:left;">
Dragon Head is a Dragonborn Echo Artifact representing the awakened skull, jaws, senses, breath, and command-presence of the First Dragons.


| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Draconic Head | Bite / Head Armor / Predator Sense Option / Breath Weapon / Roar / Stone Refresh | Echo-bound |

Dragon Head occupies the Head Slot. A Dragonborn with Dragon Head cannot wear another Head Artifact, helmet, mask, crown, or magical headgear. Dragon Head is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, or unbound.

</div>

#### Bite and Head Armor Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Bite Weapon Damage | Base Value B: Head Armor |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 1d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 2 | 2d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 3 | 3d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 4 | 4d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 5 | 5d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 6 | 6d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 7 | 7d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 8 | 8d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 9 | 9d8 | +5 Armor |
| Level 10 | 10d8 | +5 Armor |

Head Armor is minor Armor and stacks with Body Armor and Shield Armor as normal Armor resolution.

---

#### Sense Option: Predator Sense

Dragon Head grants **Predator Sense** as an available Special Sense Slot option. Dragonborn often describe this as Scent of Blood, heat on the tongue, pressure behind the eyes, or the instinctive recognition of wounded prey.

Predator Sense is separate from both Base Values and does not scale with Artifact Level. It uses the standard **20 m** range and the normal Special Sense Slot rules.

---

#### Breath Weapon Rules

Breath Weapon grants **two alternative catalogue modes** at the same Artifact Level: **Damage Breath = Melee AoE Attack** and **Ruin Breath = Melee AoE Special: Ruin**, both at the listed Power Level. Choose exactly one mode on each use. These are not a combined Power and use the same Breath Weapon use counter.

Both modes are **self-centered Martial AoE Powers** using their own exact catalogue radii. No Cone/Line geometries or separate dragon-specific damage dice are granted. Damage Breath uses Dragon Head's current **Bite Weapon Damage** as the melee Weapon Damage source and adds only the catalogue's printed Melee AoE Attack bonus Damage Dice. Ruin Breath applies only the catalogue's printed Ruin(X), without Weapon or Power damage.

Make one Martial AoE Attack Roll, compare it separately to each affected creature's Evade, and use the normal Dive for Cover and other defenses. Neither mode creates additional attacks or special riders. A successful Damage Breath may use the normal Weapon Special rules only if the source actually has such a Special; Bite has none by default.

---

}}

{{wide
#### Breath Weapon Uses

Breath Weapon can be used a number of times per Safe Haven Rest equal to half the character's Mastery Rank, rounded up.

| Mastery Rank | Breath Weapon Uses per Safe Haven Rest |
|:---:|:---:|
| MR 1 | 1 |
| MR 2 | 1 |
| MR 3 | 2 |
| MR 4 | 2 |
| MR 5 | 3 |
| MR 6 | 3 |
| MR 7 | 4 |
| MR 8 | 4 |

#### Draconic Recovery

When Dragon Head reaches Artifact Level 3, choose one Attribute for Draconic Recovery. This choice is permanent. Draconic Recovery can be used once per Safe Haven Rest and restores spent Stones only from that Attribute. Draconic Recovery II and III replace the earlier profile; they do not grant additional uses.

:::

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Unbound

The Wyld does not create species.

It creates responses.

The Unbound are those responses made flesh. They are not one people, one bloodline, or one culture. They are what happens when the world reacts to violation: when divine law is nailed into the land, when a sacred place is bent out of its nature, when hunger is manufactured, when Hell teaches the soil to rot, or when mortals are forced to become what they were never meant to be.

The Wyld responds.

But not every response wears the same shape.
Some responses become bodies of fang, horn, claw, feather, hunger, and sacred violence. These are the **Beasts**.

Some responses gather in circles, curses, roots, ash, old names, wet earth, and forbidden rites. These are the **Witches**.

Some responses remain almost human, but arm themselves with discipline, poisons, elixirs, relics, scars, and the knowledge of how to kill what should not be allowed to live. These are the **Banes**.

What binds all Unbound is not blood. It is opposition. They are made to resist what does not belong.

After the War of the Gods, when the Eight drove their order across the world and hammered Sentinels into the map like nails, many Unbound became hidden enemies of that sacred regime. They moved as saboteurs, curse-speakers, shrine-breakers, road-hunters, wilderness guardians, and cells of resistance born from forests, caverns, swamps, battlefields, and places where old wounds never closed.

Now the gods are gone, and Hell has taken their place.

The Unbound do not mourn the old order. To them, demons are only another invading pressure, another unnatural claim laid upon a wounded world. And so they fight again: against corruption, possession, false rule, branded flesh, poisoned land, and all who would teach the world to accept its own violation.

Most Unbound are not evil.

But neither are they gentle.

They are driven creatures of correction, instinct, rite, hunt, and unfinished purpose. In them, the self is often thinner than the response that made them. When they lose control, they do not simply become monsters.

They become what the Wyld made them to be.

To play an Unbound is to be the world’s response to something that should not exist.

You were not made for peace.

The question is whether you can still choose what must be broken.


| **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|
| Manifestation | Medium | 8 m |

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
##### Echo Burden: The Response Takes Over

All Unbound are responses of the Wyld.

They were shaped to oppose violation, corruption, false rule, divine control, demonic possession, and all things forced upon the world against its nature. But the response that made them is not passive. It waits beneath choice, beneath discipline, beneath identity.

When an Unbound is pushed too far, the response may take over.

This does not mean they become evil. It means they become dangerously pure. The Beast becomes the shape that hunts and tears. The Witch becomes the curse that speaks through flesh. The Bane becomes the weapon that can no longer stop hunting.

Every Unbound fears this moment, because it is powerful, useful, and sometimes necessary.

But it is never fully theirs.

---
::
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### The Beasts

Beasts are the body's response: Unbound shaped by transformation, instinct, animal memory, hunger, and sacred violence. Their power takes the form of wolf, bear, raven, stag, fox, boar, serpent, lynx, and stranger shapes remembered by the old wilds. Beneath every Beast lies **Primal Rage**, an instinctive violence they spend their lives learning to control. Most learn restraint, the difference between prey and friend, and how to remain themselves when blood, pain, and fear call the Beast forward. A Beast may choose the **Predator Crown**, embodying the hunting mind, natural weapons, and predatory senses, or the **Primal Vestment**, embodying the hunting body, supernatural recovery, and deliberately unleashed Rage. Primal Vestment is optional, but a Beast who chooses it automatically gains the **Primal Rage (2 Points)** Disadvantage. Rage Unbound represents the Beast consciously releasing this power and mechanically functions as **Extra Attack Stone Power Support**; Primal Rage represents the moment that control fails.

#### Predator Crown

Predator Crown is a Beast Unbound Echo Artifact representing the awakened hunting mind, bestial senses, fangs, beak, horns, jaws, and violent instinct of the Wyld written into the head.

It may manifest as wolfish jaws, bear teeth, raven eyes, antlers, fox ears, tusks, slit pupils, blackened gums, a second voice in the throat, or a skull-shape that becomes more visible when blood is near.

Choose one Predator Shape when this Artifact is created:

**Wolf, Bear, Raven, Stag, Fox, Boar, Serpent, Lynx, or another Beast shape approved by the GM.**

The chosen shape defines the appearance and narration of the Artifact, but all Predator Crowns use the same rules unless a specific entry says otherwise.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Bestial Head | Bite Damage / Head Armor / Predator Sense Option / Stone Power Support / Reaction: Damage / Active Buff: Damage | Echo-bound |

Predator Crown occupies the Head Slot.

A Beast with Predator Crown cannot wear another Head Artifact, helmet, mask, crown, horn Artifact, magical headgear, or similar Head-based Artifact.

Predator Crown is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

#### Predator Crown Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Bite Damage | Base Value B: Head Armor |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 4d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 2 | 5d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 3 | 6d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 4 | 7d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 5 | 8d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 6 | 9d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 7 | 10d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 8 | 11d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 9 | 12d8 | +5 Armor |
| Level 10 | 14d8 | +5 Armor |

Bite Damage is Natural Weapon Damage.

Bite Damage may represent a bite, beak strike, horn gore, tusk strike, throat snap, or similar head-based natural attack depending on the chosen Predator Shape.

Head Armor is minor Armor and stacks with Body Armor and Shield Armor as normal Armor resolution.

---

#### Sense Option: Predator Sense

Predator Crown grants **Predator Sense** as an available Special Sense Slot option.

Predator Sense is separate from both Base Values, does not scale with Artifact Level, and uses the standard **20 m** range.

Predator Sense does not read thoughts, reveal full plans, or detect creatures with no readable blood, fear, weakness, or hunting trace.

---
}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Predator Stone Choice

When Predator Crown is created, choose one Predator Stone path.

This choice is permanent.

- **Might — Damage**
- **Wits — Initiative Boost**
- **Intellect — Raises**
---

#### Primal Vestment

Primal Vestment is a Beast Unbound Echo Artifact representing the awakened hunting body: violent endurance and the primal fury normally kept beneath conscious control. It may manifest as thick fur, altered muscle, visible veins, hardened tendons, scar tissue that closes while watched, patches of hide, elongated limbs, black blood, a second heartbeat, or a body that becomes visibly less human when violence begins.

Beasts who choose the Primal Vestment are sometimes called **Ragebound**. Even among other Unbound they are often regarded with suspicion. Their power is respected, but so is the danger of losing control. Many Ragebound become loners, are kept at the edge of camps and settlements, or find that others prefer not to stand too close when blood is spilled.

| Slot | Base Type                   | Artifact Functions                                          | Binding    |
| ---- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- |
| Body | Bestial Vestment / No Armor | Healing Stone Power Support (Self only) / Rage Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Primal Vestment occupies the **Body Slot** and is Echo-bound. A Beast with Primal Vestment cannot use another Body Artifact or Body Armor.

**Primal Vestment has no Base Values.**

---

### Primal Rage *(2 Points)*

A Beast who chooses Primal Vestment automatically gains **Primal Rage** as a **2 Point Disadvantage**. These points count normally toward the character's Disadvantage Point total and Reroll Point maximum. Primal Rage cannot be removed while Primal Vestment remains part of the Beast.

You spend your life suppressing something that wants to hunt, tear, and answer pain with violence.

**Trigger:** When you lose your **second Health Level**, and each time you lose another Health Level thereafter, make a `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 6**. If one damage instance crosses several qualifying Health Levels, make one roll for each threshold crossed. No Raises may be declared.

On failure, you enter **Primal Rage** for `1d8 / 2 Rounds, rounded up`.

**While in Primal Rage:**

* Gain **+1d8 damage per Mastery Rank**.
* You must attack the nearest creature, friend or foe.
* At the end of each Round, repeat the `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 6**.

On success, Primal Rage ends.

Primal Rage never forces you to spend Stones or activate a Stone Ability.
}}

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Beast Artifact Exception

Primal Vestment is an explicit Beast Echo Artifact exception. It may contain **two Stone Power Support functions** and may provide **Extra Attack Stone Power Support from the Body Slot**, despite the normal Body Artifact restrictions. These exceptions apply only to Primal Vestment.

The Artifact-facing name **Rage Unbound** represents the Beast deliberately releasing its fury. Mechanically, it is always **Stone Ability: Extra Attack**. Rage Unbound is controlled; **Primal Rage** is what happens when that control fails.

---

### Witches
Witches are the rite’s response.
They do not simply cast spells. They bind old agreements, speak curses, call roots through stone, rot purity, sour blood, twist luck, and remember names the temples tried to bury. Witch traditions often gather around a dominant working: Root Witches bind, entangle, hold, and awaken the deep green will of the land; Ruin Witches curse, break, hex, and turn certainty into collapse; Blight Witches poison, corrode, wither, and teach flesh, metal, and faith how to decay. A Witch does not force the Wyld. A Witch gives it a shape and pays the price for being heard.

#### Witch Staff

A Witch Staff is a Witch Unbound Echo Artifact representing the old rite made into wood, bone, root, ash, iron, thorn, rot, or blackened saint-wood.

Every Witch Staff is a two-handed arcane weapon and a Spell Focus from Level 1.

Witch Staffs are not one tradition. When this Artifact is created, choose one Witch Tradition:

- **Root Staff** — Root Witch
- **Ruin Staff** — Ruin Witch
- **Blight Staff** — Blight Witch

The chosen tradition determines the Staff’s Tradition Special, Main Spell, and Paired Spell.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Two-Handed Arcane Staff | Weapon Damage / Spell Focus / Tradition Special / Raises Stone Power Support / Binding Circle (Ranged AoE Root Active) / Tradition Main Spell | Echo-bound |

Witch Staff occupies both hand Slots.

A Witch with Witch Staff cannot use another weapon, shield, hand focus, claw Artifact, or hand-based magical item at the same time.

Witch Staff is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

Witch Staff counts as a **Spell Focus** from Level 1.

Witch Staff may be used with **Intellect**.

---

### Witch Staff Traditions

| Tradition | Staff Name | Tradition Special | Artifact-Facing Main Spell | Technical Main Spell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Root Witch | Root Staff | Slow | Sapping Roots | **Ranged Single Target Special: Weaken** |
| Ruin Witch | Ruin Staff | Hex | Ruinous Word | **Ranged Single Target Special: Ruin** |
| Blight Witch | Blight Staff | Corrode | Blightcall | **Ranged Single Target Special: Blight** |

The Main Spell is granted by the Staff through its Level Progression. Each tradition also gains the same **Binding Circle** Ranged AoE Special: Root at Artifact Levels 2/5/8. Binding Circle and Main Spell are separate Actives, each using only their printed catalogue values and normal action rules. Neither Active adds damage or a second Stone Function.

The Paired Spell is not granted by the Staff. A Witch character is expected to learn and advance the Paired Spell normally if they want to fully follow that tradition.

##### Root Staff

A Root Staff is grown from buried wood, grave roots, thorn knots, wet earth, and old circles drawn under the soil.


---

##### Ruin Staff

A Ruin Staff is made from blackened wood, cracked bone, broken shrine-metal, ash, old curses, and names that were erased from temple records.

---

##### Blight Staff

A Blight Staff is swollen with fungus, rust, sour water, corpse-bloom, mold, and green-black veins of living decay.

---
}}

### Banes 

Banes are Unbound who carry the hunt into the world.

They are not Beasts who surrender to animal shape, and they are not Witches who bind themselves primarily through covenants and rites.

Banes survive through preparation, endurance, old wrath, relics, tools, scars, and the refusal to die before monsters.

When a Bane Echo is created, choose one Bane Call:

- **Alchemist**
- **Greenwarden**
- **Relic Hunter**

The chosen Call defines the Bane's method, appearance, and expected Power direction.

It does not create a separate species.
It is the shape the hunt has taken.

---

#### Alchemist


![Alchemist](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/NPC%27s/NPC/Daro-Unbound-Alchemist-noBG.png){position:absolute,top:320px,right:80px,width:400px}
<div style="width:45%; margin:left;">

Alchemists are Banes who survive through preparation. They brew potions, oils, salves, bitter draughts, tinctures, and hunter mixtures from roots, ash, venom, blood, bone, and demon-tainted remains. An Alchemist does not win by becoming a monster. 
An Alchemist studies the monster, prepares the body, coats the blade, drinks the poison first, and walks into the dark with a plan. Alchemists fit characters who want potions, oils, preparation, resistance, toxins, temporary empowerment, and controlled self-enhancement. 

Their power is preparation, not transformation.



#### Alchemist Coat

The Alchemist Coat is a Bane Echo Artifact made from hardened leather, stitched iron, bone plates, sealed vials, black glass, monster sinew, silver hooks, blood tubes, hidden needles, and reinforced potion harnesses.

It is not a robe and not a trinket.

It is real armor.

The Alchemist Coat protects the body while carrying the volatile mixtures that allow a Bane Alchemist to survive the hunt.

</div>

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Armor | Armor / Damage Potion / Alchemist Stone Artifact Function / Critical Potion | Echo-bound |

Alchemist Coat occupies the Body Slot.

A Bane with Alchemist Coat cannot wear another Body Artifact, magical armor, robe Artifact, natural armor Artifact, or body-based transformation Artifact at the same time.

Alchemist Coat is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Alchemist Coat Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Medium Armor |
|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 12 Armor |
| Level 2 | 13 Armor |
| Level 3 | 14 Armor |
| Level 4 | 15 Armor |
| Level 5 | 16 Armor |
| Level 6 | 17 Armor |
| Level 7 | 18 Armor |
| Level 8 | 19 Armor |
| Level 9 | 20 Armor |
| Level 10 | 22 Armor |

Alchemist Coat uses the normal Medium Armor drawbacks unless another rule explicitly changes them.

---

#### Alchemist Potions

Alchemist Potions are normal **Active Buffs**. 
They follow all normal Active Buff rules.
A character may normally maintain only one Active Buff at a time.
If the Alchemist activates a new Potion while already maintaining another Active Buff, the previous Active Buff ends immediately unless another rule explicitly allows both to coexist.
The Potion name is only the Artifact's flavour name.
The mechanical Power is the listed existing Active Buff.

---

#### Red Potion

Red Potion floods the body with heat, rage, pressure, and violent focus.
Mechanically, Red Potion uses the normal **Active Buff: Damage**.

---

#### Black Potion

Black Potion sharpens the senses until every pulse, blink, tendon, and weak point becomes visible.
Mechanically, Black Potion uses the normal **Active Buff: Critical**.

---

#### Alchemist Stone

Alchemist Stone is a dedicated **Artifact Function** of the Alchemist Coat, not an Attribute Stone Ability. It improves one **Red Potion** or **Black Potion** Active Buff when that Potion is activated.

At Artifact Level 2, 5, or 8, the effect is respectively **+1 / +2 / +3 effective Power Levels** and **+1 / +2 / +3 Rounds**. These values replace earlier Alchemist Stone values; they do not add together.

The normal Active Buff rules still apply. The effective Power Level cannot exceed the applicable Power Level cap, and Alchemist Stone does not allow a second Active Buff to be maintained. If another source empowers the same Buff, use only the highest applicable increase unless an explicit rule permits stacking.
---
}}

#### Greenwarden


![Greenwarden](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/NPC%27s/NPC/Daro-Unbound-Alchemist-noBG.png){position:absolute,top:20px,right:80px,width:400px}
<div style="width:45%; margin:left;">

Greenwardens are Banes touched by the old green wrath of the world.
They are inspired by ancient forest guardians, thorn-covered wanderers, antlered figures, bark-skinned protectors, moss-grown bodies, and the idea that nature itself remembers what was done to it.
A Greenwarden is not a Beast.
They do not become an animal.
They become something older: bark, thorn, moss, antler, sap, rot, root, and patient violence.
Greenwardens fit characters who want thorns, reflected harm, bark-skin, close-range punishment, defensive retaliation, and the feeling that the world itself answers every wound.
Their power is endurance and retaliation.

---

#### Green Warden Mantle

The Green Warden Mantle is a Bane Echo Artifact grown from bark, thorn, moss, old roots, green iron, beast hide, sacred soil, and the last living strength of the wild places.
It is not a druidic robe and not a holy vestment.
It is the armor of a protector.
Green Wardens do not serve gods.
They stand between the living world and the things that would harvest it.

</div>

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Armor | Armor / Active Buff: Thorns / Reaction: Ally Armor / Special Negation Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Green Warden Mantle occupies the Body Slot.

A Green Warden with Green Warden Mantle cannot wear another Body Artifact, magical armor, robe Artifact, natural armor Artifact, or body-based transformation Artifact at the same time.

Green Warden Mantle is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

### Green Warden Mantle Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Medium Armor |
|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 12 Armor |
| Level 2 | 13 Armor |
| Level 3 | 14 Armor |
| Level 4 | 15 Armor |
| Level 5 | 16 Armor |
| Level 6 | 17 Armor |
| Level 7 | 18 Armor |
| Level 8 | 19 Armor |
| Level 9 | 20 Armor |
| Level 10 | 22 Armor |

Green Warden Mantle uses the normal Medium Armor drawbacks unless another rule explicitly changes them.

---
}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Green Warden Passive Access

A Green Warden may choose the **Thorns Passive** during character creation.
Thorns Passive is exclusive to Green Wardens unless another rule explicitly grants access.
The Green Warden must still spend one Passive Slot to slot Thorns Passive.
If Thorns Passive is not slotted, it provides no benefit.
The **Thorns Active** is not exclusive. Any character may learn the Thorns Active if they meet its normal requirements.
A Green Warden may have both the Thorns Passive and the Thorns Active.

---

### Green Warden Thorns

Green Warden Mantle grants access to the normal **Active Buff: Thorns**.
Active Buff: Thorns represents thorns, roots, bark splinters, hooked vines, green iron spines, or living brambles striking back at nearby enemies.
The name is the Artifact's flavour name.
The mechanical Power is the existing **Active Buff: Thorns**.

---

### Green Warden Protection

Green Warden Mantle grants access to the normal **Reaction: Ally Armor**.
This Reaction represents roots rising, bark hardening, thorns catching a blow, or the Warden forcing the living world between an ally and harm.
The Reaction follows all normal Reaction rules.
It applies only to the triggering ally hit or damage instance.
It does not create a maintained state.
It is not an Active Buff.

---

### Green Warden Stone

Green Warden Mantle supports the **Resolve Ability: Special Negation** Stone Power.
The Mantle pre-fills higher tiers of that Stone Power through its Level Progression.
The character must still pay all required lower tiers normally.


### Level Progression

#### Hunter's Scourge

![Greenwarden](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/NPC%27s/NPC/Daro-Unbound-Alchemist-noBG.png){position:absolute,top:20px,right:80px,width:400px}
<div style="width:45%; margin:left;">

Hunter's Scourge is a Relic Hunter Echo Artifact: a chain-whip, iron lash, hooked cord, weighted scourge, relic whip, or segmented killing chain built to drag monsters into reach and destroy them.
Relic Hunters are not holy knights.
They are trained killers of demons, undead, fiends, cursed things, and monsters that survived too long.
Where Alchemists survive the hunt by changing their own bodies, and Green Wardens protect what still lives, Relic Hunters end the hunt by pulling the monster close and burning it out of the world.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Two-Handed Chain Whip | Whip Damage / Finesse / Reach / Limited Single Target Strike / Expose Special / Damage Maximization Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Hunter's Scourge occupies both hand Slots.
A Relic Hunter with Hunter's Scourge cannot use another weapon, shield, hand focus, claw Artifact, or hand-based magical item at the same time.
Hunter's Scourge is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

</div>

Hunter's Scourge uses the normal **Whip** as its Base Weapon.

Hunter's Scourge turns this weapon into a two-handed Echo Artifact.

It keeps **Finesse** and **Reach (+1 m)** from the Base Weapon.

### Level Progression

**Limited source:** Scourge Strike is a granted variant of the normal **Melee Single Target Attack**. It inherits that catalogue Power's full Attack Roll, action, delivery, and other rules. The normal Power bonus Damage Dice are doubled by the universal Limited rule; the weapon's Base Damage is not. Its use counter scales with effective Power Level and resets only on a Safe Haven Rest. It grants no free additional Attack Actions, and the Artifact's copy does not bypass duplicate-source restrictions.

**Hunter's Scourge Stone Support exception:** Although Hand Artifacts normally grant Might/Agility-oriented Stone support, Hunter's Scourge is an explicitly approved Echo Artifact exception supporting **Vitality: Damage Maximization**. This grants no Vitality Stones or automatic access to locked Vitality Stone Abilities: the wielder still needs the Ability legally unlocked and must pay its required lower Ranks. This exception applies only to the named Hunter's Scourge support, not to Hand Artifacts generally.

**Damage Maximization Support:** Scourge Strike is a **Limited Instant Active** and **may be maximized** by **Vitality Ability: Damage Maximization**. Hunter's Scourge grants the normal Stone Power Support for this Ability at Artifact Levels 2 / 5 / 8. Support pre-fills exactly the named Rank's incremental Stone cost; lower Ranks remain payable. Choose the Rank and pay the remaining cost once during the first Preparation Phase of combat. The Support does not grant a second activation, extra Damage Rolls, additional Limited uses, or a separate Power. A maximization may be spent on Scourge Strike or on any other Damage Roll the wielder makes, following the universal Damage Maximization rules.

The 16d8 is the Limited output of its normal 8d8 Power bonus; the Scourge's Weapon Damage is not doubled. |
| **2** | **Damage Maximization Support I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Vitality Ability: Damage Maximization (Stone Power Support, Rank 2)</span> | Stone Power Support | Self | — | Instant | Pre-fill **Rank 2** of **Vitality: Damage Maximization** (**2-Stone Rank cost** supplied by Hunter's Scourge).
